| 159 | **TTS: interword hyphens read as pauses — "half-buried" spoke as "half … buried" (field report 2026-08-10).** TLDR: hyphenated compounds (half-buried, cargo-culting) paused mid-word on the Piper-class tiers; the hyphen now becomes a space before the engine ever sees it, so compounds read as adjacent words while the comma/period rhythm the user likes stays untouched. **Mechanism (grounded on the t1593 corpus):** the GM writes compounds with plain ASCII hyphens — 1011 instances across 1.8MB of prose, in 636 of 1592 GM entries (40% of all narration), and ZERO em/en dashes anywhere — but `normalizeForTTS` only ever handled `--` and em/en dashes, and the unit splitter never breaks at hyphens, so the compound reached Piper intact and espeak-ng phonemized the hyphen as an audible pause INSIDE the synthesized wav — the one place the scheduled inter-unit gaps (comma 0.15s / full stop 0.44s) can't reach. **Fix (tts.js):** one lookahead rule in `normalizeForTTS` — `([A-Za-z])-(?=[A-Za-z])` → letter+space; the unconsumed trailing letter means chained compounds ("three-and-a-half") strip every link. Scope deliberately letters-only: digit ranges ("3-4"), spaced breaks (" - "), and the `--`/em-dash dashRepl rules untouched; unconditional across engines (OS voices speak both forms identically, so native is neutral and the paths can't drift). | S | Any | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.585** (2026-08-10). 2 failing-first tests + 2 pins (scope boundaries; B14 unit-count invariance — persisted speaker maps key on counts) → 1199 ALL GREEN; real-data check through the shipped pipeline: 5 real hyphen-bearing t1593 entries → 232 units, zero surviving interword hyphens, zero unit-count changes. piper_test.html's spike-era normalize copy left as-is (historical, pre-harvest, already drifted). |

| 156 | **DRIFT HARDENING: THE IDENTITY LAYER — one standardized identity scheme across every name-keyed store (absorbs #152 + #153 + #155; direction ruled 2026-08-09).** TLDR: the engine keys ALL canon by model-authored name strings and treats string equality as identity — so every store eventually FRAGMENTS (many names, one thing) or FUSES (one name, many things); all four NPC/location quadrants are field-confirmed at t1593 (three Savahs fused into one record · Hemlock variants (#128, the shipped template) · one "Varisia - North Road" node carrying two different roads, causing the Frizwick phantom rejoin · the 8-node Sandpoint fragmentation cloud), and five stores have grown independent partial guards (the enumerate-the-class rule tripped at instance five). **Full proposal: [DOC/identity_hardening_fable.html](DOC/identity_hardening_fable.html)** — problem statement first, then the design: an `IDENTITY_DOMAINS` registry (ONE uniform adapter per store — the PROVIDERS/BIBLE_TYPES/TAG_TABLE house pattern), ONE shared resolver, ONE two-direction detector (drift token-overlap + collision context-mismatch; GM-decides nudges, NEVER auto-merge/auto-block), ONE generalized `[ALIAS:domain\|canonical\|alias]` / `[MERGE:domain\|canonical\|duplicate]` action pair (legacy NPC_ALIAS/NPC_MERGE stay as thin routes into the same handlers), ONE suggestion-pool prevention seam (the pool currently offers "Frizwick Coldwater" with Frizwick in the party), ONE stable-half NAMING clause (with the WHY — the model doesn't know names are keys). | 147 | **DRIFT HARDENING: corrections survive their own retcon — pin the corrected truth until memory files it.** TLDR: [RETCON:] de-indexes the correction along with the mistake; a bounded pin keeps the corrected fact injected until the next summarize lands, then archives. <br><br>**Solution:** the `[RETCON:]` observation site (logTranscript's rc-marking, state.js) also sets `worldState.retconPin = {what: <the tag's own payload text>, turn}` — the payload already states what was corrected, no prose excerpting. `buildSysPrompt` (volatile half) injects one line while the pin lives: "CORRECTION IN FORCE (tN): <what> — the corrected telling is canon; the earlier one is void." | 146 | **DRIFT HARDENING: clock repairs become idempotent transactions; impossible timelines get caught at load.** TLDR: the −1160 repair landed twice (−2320, transcript-proven); repairs get receipts riding the save, and load warns on born>now or regressed stamps. <br><br>**Solution:** ① `clockRepair(repairId, expectedMin, delta)` (clock.js): applies ONLY when `clock.min===expectedMin` AND `repairId` is absent from `worldState.clock.repairs[]` — the receipt list rides the save/sync blob, so a second device sees the first application (cross-device blindness IS the double-fire vector; a device-local receipt would not have prevented this); mismatch or duplicate → loud toast + console, zero mutation. ② `migrateWorldState` invariant check: any schedule with `born > clock.min` or `due < born` → console.warn + one-shot toast naming the anomaly; NEVER auto-heal (transcript stamps are decree-immutable — quarantine-and-ask only). ③ THIS campaign — **adjudication RULED 2026-08-08 (user): the intended timeline is ONE −1160 repair, so restore +1160 once** (true min = current+1160; both schedule anchors then sit legitimately in the past, the Sable window returns to ~18h). After ① ships, run `clockRepair("142-double-decrement", <current min>, 1160)` on ONE device as the build's closing step — the ruling authorizes the run; execution is part of finishing this row, not a separate ask. Red-first: refuse-on-mismatch, refuse-on-duplicate, invariant warn fires on the t1549 fixture, silent on healthy saves. <br><br>**Evidence (audit round):** the interim #142 repair was a raw console decrement (`clock.min-=1160`) with no expected-before value, repair id, idempotency, or invariant check. The latest t1549 save proves it ran twice or had an exactly equivalent second edit: GM clock stamps fall from 8820 at t1525 to 6500 at t1526, precisely **−2320 = 2×1160**, while turn numbers remain monotonic and normal engine code only increments `clock.min`. Current `clock.min=6680`, but both surviving schedules have `born` in the future (6915 and 7490), which violates the clock contract and extends the Sable deadline by 810 minutes relative to its creation duration. `migrateWorldState` only shape-heals the clock; it does not check transcript-clock monotonicity or `born <= now <= due`. Scope is honest: this is a repair/migration safety defect, not a normal-turn regression. Candidate direction: preconditioned/idempotent repair transactions plus load-time clock/schedule invariant diagnostics; long term, derive/replay corrections from a ledger rather than editing the scalar. | M | Fable | 🧪 **BUILT v1.566** (2026-08-08, drift pass order 3) — **the ONE remaining step is running the repair on the live device.** Shipped: `clockRepair(repairId,expectedMin,delta)` (clock.js) — refuses on receipt-id match (receipts ride `worldState.clock.repairs[]` in the sync blob, so the second device sees the first application — the double-fire vector) and on expected-state mismatch, both loud, zero mutation on refusal; `clockTimelineAnomalies()` (pure) + migrateWorldState wiring warns on EVERY load while born>now or due<born stands — never auto-heals. 5 red-first tests → green (1094); **full arc proven on a scratch copy of the real t1549 save**: both anomalies warn at load → `clockRepair("142-double-decrement", 6680, 1160)` applies 6680→7840 with receipt → the second application REFUSES → anomaly list empty. **▶ USER ACTION (ruled + authorized): on ONE device, desktop console:** `clockRepair("142-double-decrement", clockNow(), 1160)` — note the deficit is constant (−1160) even though min keeps advancing with play, so the receipt (not expectedMin) is the load-bearing guard for this particular repair; expectedMin earns its keep on future repairs prescribed with a written-down value. Consolidated 2026-08-08: Sol R3 — Fable conceded its earlier rejection (the transcript ck stamps prove the double application; re-verified independently) — [SOL](DOC/Drift_risks_SOL.html) §R3 · [FABLE](DOC/Drift_risks_FABLE.html) comments. Do NOT auto-repair this campaign before the instrument exists. |

The carve-out is automatic: a player action naming a split member is story text and still activates detail (the SPLIT THREADS design needs it to advance remote threads). Sol's story-only hot set + typed provenance spans stay recorded as the end state — deliberately NOT this pass. <br><br>**Evidence (audit round):** `hotNpcs` scans the raw last six `sessionLog` messages, including `[ENGINE NOTE ...]` text, while `stripEngineNotes` is applied only in the summarize path. Counterweight: the later structured split block is correct and more explicit, so this is conflict pressure, not proof the model must choose wrong. | 144 | **DRIFT HARDENING: NPC knowledge lifecycle — archive every eviction, converge over-caps, keep newest on render; then type the facts.** TLDR: NPC knowledge is the only memory tier that silently destroys canon — evictions, merges, truncation; archive every loss, then split durable canon from scene chatter. <br><br>**Solution — Phase A (mechanical; the bleeding stops):** ① both write-site caps (memory.js:976 summarize path, :940 supersede path) stop `.shift()`-ing to the void — evicted fact → `memArchive().npcKnowledge.push({npc,fact,turn})`, with `npcKnowledge` added to the memArchive/healMemory self-heal set; ② the `[NPC_MERGE:]` re-slice (tag_table.js:318) archives its truncated overflow + loud console.warn + a muts line ("merge truncated N facts — archived"); ③ `fileNpcEvent`'s events cap-8 slice archives the same way; ④ load-time convergence in `migrateWorldState`: knowledge length>12 → archive the OLDEST overflow, keep newest 12, one console.info per NPC (heals Frizwick 18→12 with 6 facts archived, not destroyed); ⑤ the render truncation (`memoryNpcDetail`, the 2000-char cut) flips to keep the NEWEST whole facts, prefixed "(N older facts omitted — archived)"; ⑥ the ui-files.js import whitelist gains `archive.npcKnowledge` AND the already-dropped `archive.superseded` (Sol's find — imports silently lose it today). Red-first per clause; the t1549 fixture proves Frizwick converges and Morwen's carved-entities fact would have survived. **Phase B (design, DRIFT PASS order 8, after A ships):** extractor schema `knowledgeGained` → `{fact, kind:"durable"/"scene"}` — scene-kind routes to turn-stamped events, never standing Knows; `memoryNpcDetail` gains a conservative conflict gate (suppress position claims contradicting `splitLoc`/current node). Model-facing extractor change → money-turn validation (the gpt-4o precedent); Phase A's archive is the safety net for any misclassification. <br><br>**Evidence (audit round, both reports):** Fable's longitudinal diff — 83 knowledge facts destroyed t1265→t1549 (Sol's exact-text variant counts 115), including Morwen's active-arc carved-entities fact, while lore/decisions/chapters lost ZERO (their P12 archive discipline works); the #128 merges cut Morwen's ~36-fact three-key history to exactly 12, archive-less. Sol's prompt reconstruction — the extractor schema offers one untyped `knowledgeGained` string; `applySummaryExtract` stores it permanently, and `memoryNpcDetail` labels the whole array `Knows:` with no turn, validity, or fact kind. The latest t1549 save contains direct contradictions: Morwen is currently with Ammut en route to Savah, but her injected detail says she “is now stationed outside the sealed door” and “remained outside”; Daeris is split at the ironworks but her detail says “Present at the carved door” and “Arrived at the workshop”; Frizwick is split at the ironworks but her detail says she “is holding the chokepoint”. Frizwick also has **18 knowledge entries despite the cap of 12**; the write boundary sheds only one entry per write, so a legacy overfill stays overfilled, while the 2,000-character renderer truncates from the tail and therefore preserves the oldest/stalest claims while hiding newer facts. Reconstructing the real t1549 prompt proves these lines are actually served, not merely stored. Counterweight: `splitLoc`, `lastSeenAt`, and the split geography block are currently correct and mitigate the conflict; identity fragmentation did not reproduce. Candidate direction: typed/temporal memory facts, scene-state facts routed to events/RAG rather than standing knowledge, load-time cap convergence, and render-time structured-state conflict filtering. | M | Fable | ✅ **Phase A SHIPPED v1.564** (2026-08-08, drift pass order 1): all six clauses landed — both write-site caps + the merge re-slice + fileNpcEvent evict to `memory.archive.npcKnowledge`/`npcEvents` (merge warns + muts line); healMemory converges over-cap lists (oldest→archive, newest 12 kept, idempotent — proven on the REAL t1549 save: Frizwick 18→12, 6 archived intact); memoryNpcDetail sheds OLDEST whole facts under the 2000-char budget with a "(N older facts not shown)" marker (P8 hard-cut kept as backstop; row text said "— archived" but render-only truncation doesn't archive — the facts stay live, deliberate wording fix); the import whitelist carries the FULL archive incl. the pre-existing superseded/coreMemories/expiredSchedules drops. 6 red-first tests → green (1088); #144A ARCHIVE CARRY CONTRACT (run-tests.js) pins the whitelist + no-bare-shift, whitelist clause sabotage-proven by name, write sites guarded by the behavioral tests (a grep clause can be evaded by variable aliasing — the engine tests are the load-bearing guard there, proven tripping). ✅ **Phase B SHIPPED v1.572** (2026-08-08, drift pass order 8): the extraction schema now teaches `knowledgeGained` as `{fact, kind}` — durable (standing truth) vs scene (true only in the moment); apply-side routes scene facts through `fileNpcEvent` as turn-stamped history, never standing Knows; unknown kind defaults durable (safe now that #144A archives evictions); the legacy string shape stays byte-compatible, so a model ignoring the new schema degrades to today's behavior, never breaks. The conflict gate shipped as the ZERO-false-positive form: `memoryNpcDetail` leads a split party member's block with an authoritative "Currently: AWAY from the party at X — claims below that contradict this are STALE" line — pure addition, no suppression (a misclassifying suppressor would hide TRUE canon, the rebuttal-round objection). 3 red-first tests → green (1118); schema-teach clause pinned in the #144A contract. ✅ **Money-turn VALIDATED 2026-08-09** (live probe on the player key, rider on the #148 gate run — [audits/AUDIT_recall_gate_v1.573.md](audits/AUDIT_recall_gate_v1.573.md)): the model emitted exactly the typed shape with correct classification on the first try — Wyla's watcher arrangement filed `durable`, her sealing-a-box moment and Morwen's in-progress errand filed `scene`. The probe's own parse hiccup was an instrument artifact (mini-prompt lacked the real pipeline's B11 note-stripping + anti-markdown enforcement; summarize() carries both + the 3-strike retry). Field watch: the first real summarize on v1.572+ should show scene facts landing as dated history, not Knows growth. | Consolidated 2026-08-08 from the dual audit + rebuttal round: Fable Finding 1 (write-side destruction, longitudinally proven) + Sol R1 (read-side truncation + temporal typing) are the two halves of ONE defect — [FABLE](DOC/Drift_risks_FABLE.html) · [SOL](DOC/Drift_risks_SOL.html). |
| 141 | **Suggestion validation pass — the in-call scene check (the driverless-cart phantom).** TLDR: a button offered "watch the driver's hands" at a cart the narration had explicitly left driverless — a generic ROLE NOUN no closed-vocabulary #126 gate rule can test (roster/spells/nodes are closed classes; "the driver" is open-class scene fact). Volatile-only (stable cache identity suite-green; the un-starve fence test's format clause updated array→object with the same guard intent). | S | Fable | 🧪 **Ready to test — built v1.559**, 2026-08-07 — field-watch phase: judge via the `[actions] scene check` console lines + whether phantom-entity buttons recur. **Escalation path if the in-call check measurably fails: the second-call verifier** (scene + 3 candidates → validate/rewrite, Haiku-class or active model — design in this row's history). 3 red-first tests (1076 total). |
| 136 | **DRIFT HARDENING: tag receipts tell the truth — the tolerant-parser counter-sweep (five defect classes).** TLDR: parsers mis-target, silently no-op, or report mutations that never happened; sweep all five classes to warn-or-documented-exemption under one pass. <br><br>*(Originally filed from the #77 nonsense-filter Fable review, [DOC/DOC_nonsense_filter.html](DOC/DOC_nonsense_filter.html) §3a; adopted into the drift pass 2026-08-08 — Sol R4 independently reproduced ①–④ by probe and added ⑤.)* The census of our sense-adjacent model-output parsers probed the loose-match boundaries and found five asymmetries where the house no-silent-failures / never-guess principles are violated — each hand-verified against the tree, none fixed ride-along because all touch the drift surface or a ratified ruling. ① **Sequential-act `[ARC_COMPLETE:]` ignores the title** (tag_table.js:648 gates the title check on `parallel`) — a hallucinated/misspelled title still closes the active arc and auto-activates the next, silently, while sibling `[ARC_CONTINUE:]` strictly validates + warns; decide design-or-oversight, then either validate-with-warn or record the ruling in a comment. ② **`combatFoeByName` containment picks the first of multiple matching foes with no warn** (tag_table.js:151; "Guard" over {Guard Captain, Guard Sergeant}) — the bare-tag path warns on exactly this ambiguity, the named path doesn't. ③ **Silent-miss cluster:** unknown `[SKILL_SUCCESS:]` id, player `[SPELL_USED:]` miss (companion twin warns — asymmetry intent unrecorded), all `COMPANION_*` unknown-name misses, `[SPELL_DEF:]` junk tier→0 + silent re-definition drop — candidate no-silent-failures violations; triage each to warn-or-documented-exemption. ④ **`[NPC_FORGET:]` has no archive** while its sibling `[NPC_SUPERSEDE:]` archives (and both retire EVERY substring-matching fact — a broad needle over-reaches; supersede is recoverable, forget is not). ⑤ **Receipts without mutations** (Sol R4, live at t1530): `[ITEM_LOST:none]` pushed the muts line "-none" though `removeInventoryItem` returned false — the handler reported success it never verified; sweep every handler that pushes a muts line without checking its helper's return value (the tagLog ring already pairs tags with muts lines, so a dev-mode verifier is a cheap diff over an existing structure). **Rebuttal-round rulings, baked in:** the ④ fix is the ARCHIVE (a tombstone in memory.archive the fiction can't see but the operator can recover) — NOT match-narrowing, because Oubliate's breadth is the spell's contract; and fail-closed is a PER-TAG declared policy, never a blanket resolver rule — bare `[ENEMY_HP:]`'s always-lands routing is ratified design and stays. | M | Fable | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.571** (2026-08-08, drift pass order 10 — 22 patches, every one target-asserted). ① sequential-act `[ARC_COMPLETE:]` validates its title (RULED oversight — the strict sibling proves the intent; no-match warns, nothing closes; the #127 escalation re-demands if genuinely done under another name). ② `combatFoeByName` warns on ambiguous containment, first still wins (always-lands ruling preserved). ③ the silent-miss cluster is loud: unknown `[SKILL_SUCCESS:]` id, player `[SPELL_USED:]` miss (asymmetry with the companion twin closed), ALL 9 silent `findCompanionChar` guards (6 bare + 3 field, generic sweep), `[SPELL_DEF:]` junk tier AND write-once redefinition drop, `[SAVE_MOD_REMOVED:]` no-op. ④ `[NPC_FORGET:]` archives everything it removes to `memory.archive.npcForgotten` (tombstone — never injected, the fiction still forgets; breadth untouched per the Oubliate ruling; whitelist + contract keys carried). ⑤ receipts without mutations are dead: ITEM_LOST (the live t1530 "-none" class), COMPANION_ITEM_LOST, CONDITION_REMOVED miss-warn, RELATIONSHIP_REMOVED (receipt AND bondToast gated), COMPANION_CONDITION/RELATIONSHIP_REMOVED — a muts line now means a mutation happened. 5 red-first tests → green (1115); pre-existing tests exercised the new warn paths without breaking (the Frost Lance/Ekene lines in the run log). |
| 128 | **NPC identity fragmentation — one person, many memory entries.** TLDR: the same character accumulates separate memory entries under name variants — the live Runelords save has 62 memory entries for 37 actual NPCs, with Sheriff Hemlock existing four times ("Sheriff Belor Hemlock" / "Sheriff Hemlock" / "Belor Hemlock" / "Hemlock"), so his history is split four ways: recall pulls from whichever fragment a scene happens to match, and every fragment looks thinner than the person really is. Confirmed by the ChatGPT gameplay review ([review](ChatGPT_REVIEW/gameplayReview_Rise_of_the_Runelords_t1385.html), finding 3 — [response](ChatGPT_REVIEW/gameplayReview_Rise_of_the_Runelords_t1385_RESPONSE.html) beside it) and reproduced on the t1265 export (61 keys / 36 NPCs). The existing tools (`[NPC_ALIAS:]`, `[NPC_MERGE:]`, the #57 merge-confirm nudge) are the right shape but under-fire — they wait for the GM or the summarize extractor to notice a duplicate. **Near term:** a deterministic variant scan — when one NPC name is contained in another ("Hemlock" ⊂ "Sheriff Belor Hemlock"), queue the pair through the existing #57 GM-confirmed merge channel so duplicates drain continuously; never auto-merge (twins, titles, and shared surnames are real people). **Long term:** stop using display names as the storage key and give every NPC a permanent internal ID — the indexing approach the user suggested back when (recorded in Architecture decisions ▸ "Stable entity IDs", 2026-06); that ruling deferred IDs until something forces them, and this field evidence is the strongest pressure yet — networked multiplayer / the server-canonical model remains the natural landing spot. **Ratified target shape (user design, 2026-08-03): a pointer table** — name keys are POINTERS to a stable numeric ID that owns the data (`"hemlock"→12345`, `"sheriff_hemlock"→12345`, `12345→{…}`), so a merge is one pointer write, a rename is one attribute flip, two NPCs can share a display name, and write-time stamps (RAG entity index) never rot. Recorded so it isn't re-litigated at migration time; the near-term variant scan is required under either storage scheme (the pointer table records "same person" cheaply but can't DECIDE it). ⚠ Drift surface (memory tiers + alias resolution) — Fable tier, design review before code. | M | Fable | 🔶 **Near-term SHIPPED v1.528** (2026-08-03): `npcVariantTokens`/`npcVariantPairs`/`scanNpcNameVariants` (memory.js) run every summarize, feeding the existing #57 queue via the extracted shared `_queueMergeHint` (once-ever latch + dedupe, one implementation for both producers). Deliberately does NOT reuse `npcCoreTokens` — that stripper drops role nouns (man/woman/stranger), safe only under resolve-time's exactly-one-candidate guard; at proposal time those words are identity-bearing. Ambiguity rules: a bare surname matching two different fuller names proposes nothing (the Perdrath-sisters case); a party member is always the canonical side. Field-verified against the live t1265 export: **12 proposals, 12 true duplicates, zero false positives** (Scarred Wolf / Marta / the Stranger correctly untouched). Test-first: 8 engine tests failing → green (988 total). **Long-term (pointer-table stable IDs) still open** — lands with the server-canonical/networked-multiplayer schema moment. |

| 115 | **Opt-in session-only provider-key storage — shrink the stored-credential surface.** TLDR: provider/fal keys and the server token are long-lived localStorage values with no CSP, so any same-origin script injection is credential theft (review finding 2); the known injection sinks were closed + contract-pinned as v1.520, but the stored-key exposure itself is an accepted trade-off of the BYO-key/no-server design. Near-term: an opt-in "don't remember my keys" mode (sessionStorage, re-entered per browser session). Long-term (the real fix, already the roadmap): the subscription-model server proxy — the PROVIDERS table is shaped as the server-side routing table for exactly this. A strict CSP only becomes feasible after the inline-onclick convention is revisited; a CSP with unsafe-inline would be security theater. | M | Opus | Not started (queued from the review response, 2026-08-02). |
| 114 | **Lazy-load heavy optional surfaces — first-launch cost on low-end phones.** TLDR: index.html eagerly loads 30 scripts (~1.5 MiB) in strict manual order; repeat loads are free (SW cache) but first-launch parse/main-thread cost grows with every release (review finding 6). Adopt incremental lazy-loading — dynamic script injection after gameplay is interactive — for genuinely optional surfaces; TTS is the first candidate (the Piper wasm runtime already defers this way; extend the pattern). ⚠ Ruled OUT: native ES modules — module scripts are CORS-blocked on file://, a supported workflow (review response, finding 6 counterpoint). | M | Opus | Not started (queued from the review response, 2026-08-02). |

Test-first (3 red → green): `pcEffectiveLoc`/`partySplitMembers` (helpers.js), one TAG_TABLE entry `[PARTY_SPLIT:Name\|Loc\|Sub?]`/`\|rejoin` (hero/unknown/dead = loud no-ops; map bookkeeping additive — node+edge+lastSeenAt, never the primary camera), SPLIT THREADS geo section every turn (F4) with the F2 move-only-via instruction, HUD camera follows the spotlight PC, "· Location" chips on party surfaces, split-aware suggestion POV, part-ways ends the thread. Suite 645 green. **ENGINE PHASES P1–P5 ALL BUILT; P4 GATE CLOSED (v1.362, 2026-07-18):** the D10 field test ran (real key, 2 PCs, user-run) — "you" was clear but ruled WRONG by design (privileges one player). **D12 supersedes D10: full THIRD-person narration for all PCs in multi-PC rounds** (round-rules line + post-STYLE override so end-position beats the stable role block's second-person; position contract engine-tested; single-player byte-identity intact — "you" returns at playerCount 1). **D12 prose FIELD-VALIDATED same day ("Third person was the right call. Feels perfect") + exit bug — 3 rounds, fixed v1.372:** demoting back to 1 PC left the narration third-person. TLDR: the instruction was in the wrong CHANNEL, not the wrong place. Rounds 1–2 (v1.368 mid-volatile, v1.370 post-STYLE) both failed because the system prompt precedes the whole conversation while the retained tail holds ~3 exchanges of the GM's own third-person prose — recency beats position, so no spot inside the system prompt could win. **Round 3: `buildMpEndNote` as an 11th NOTE_BUILDERS entry** — the directive rides the USER message (newest tokens, after the third-person output), the channel built for precisely this class; `ENGINE_NOTES_PROTOCOL` got a prose-directive carve-out or it would have suppressed the note by the same rule. `worldState.mpEnded` armed by demote toggles (hero-is-sole-PC guarded), 3-turn window, re-promote cancels. **HOT-SEAT MULTIPLAYER COMPLETE (user, 2026-07-20): the multi-human session ran and "it works."** F3 combat tripwire + misroute-toast noise held up in real play. **Q5 (Car Mode × multiplayer) RESOLVED → D13** (spec): the visual hand-off (spotlight portrait swap, P2) is a sufficient turn indicator — no audio cue; who-reads-aloud was already answered by D12 (third person → one reader); the two voice-input hazards (STT nonsense + speaker-misattribution) are delegated OUT of #1 to **#77** (confirm-before-send read-back, which backstops misattribution as a side effect) and **#78** (numbered read-back). **Only Q7 remains — networked co-op / per-device, the full XL version (separate devices, live sync), explicitly OUT of the hot-seat first pass; stable entity IDs fold in there (server-canonical state is what forces them), not here.** So the hot-seat build is closed; #1 stays open only as the networked-multiplayer tracker. |
| 5 | Story compiler — weave the campaign into a cohesive short story the player can keep and read later. PDF final, HTML fallback. | L | Opus | Planning doc written ([STORY_COMPILER.md](DOC/STORY_COMPILER.md)) — chunked compile, standalone HTML first, ~5.5h build estimate. **DECISIONS (2026-06-15):** ① **Weave from VERBATIM PROSE, not chapter summaries** — the keepsake must preserve the actual told story (real dialogue / GM prose), not re-inflated summaries (this supersedes the doc's "memory.chapters is the spine"). ② Architecture: complete append-only **transcript = the flesh** (source prose), `memory.chapters` + `storyBeats` + `keyDecisions` = the **skeleton** (arc shape, pacing, and which transcript stretches to pull verbatim). ③ **Hard dependency — blocked on #25:** needs a complete/ordered/durable transcript that doesn't exist yet; the auto-export `.txt` files can't serve (desktop-only trigger, DOM-snapshot truncated by reload, scattered per-device). Build transcript capture FIRST. ④ Cost: skeleton must *select* transcript stretches, not feed all ~1200 turns. Chunking/voice/output sections of the doc still valid; only the input source changed. **Build parked** (user's call). <br>**ARCHITECTURE REVISION (2026-07-09) — pipeline of composable POST-PROCESSES, not a monolithic compile.** The transcript dependency (#25/#12) is now DONE, and **Export Narrative shipped v1.229** (`buildNarrativeHtml`, File ▸ Save/Load ▸ 📜 Export Narrative) — transcript → styled HTML with player actions as `.act` blocks and GM prose as `.gm` blocks. That structured, free, deterministic export is **stage 1** and the enabler for everything after it: because the export tags which text is player vs GM in the markup, later passes parse it back into `(action, narration)` pairs and run on the **saved file** — no live campaign/transcript needed, reusable on any exported chronicle. **Stage 2+ = optional, composable LLM passes, each producing a SEPARATE doc (never overwrite the raw chronicle; it stays the honest source of truth):** <br>• **Fold player actions** — weave each player action into the following narration so it reads as continuous prose. The core rule (from play): the drop-vs-fold decision is **per-seam, not global** — if the following narration already conveys the action, DROP it; if it assumes the action without showing it (leaving an incongruous hole), FOLD a brief clause in; preserve the GM's actual prose/dialogue, smooth seams only, invent nothing. LLM judgment per transition, not string surgery. <br>• **Person conversion (2nd/1st → 3rd)** — recast the immersive second-person "you" (+ first-person player actions) into a third-person novelization ("Vyrindra…/she…", named from the sheet + gender). **LLM, not regex** — the trap is dialogue: narration-"you" must convert but an NPC *saying* "you" must stay. Pairs naturally with a **present→past** tense shift (novel feel). A **taste choice**, not a default — some want immersive 2nd-person, some want a novel about their character. <br>**Both passes still need:** chunking (fold/convert a chapter at a time, reassemble — cost + context limits) and the per-seam/per-line model judgment; the post-process framing just isolates all that difficulty behind an opt-in. **Load-bearing constraint:** the export must keep its block structure MACHINE-READABLE (`.act`/`.gm` classes) — flattening it to undifferentiated prose would break every downstream pass. Order matters (fold → then person). |

Build order ruled by the review: L0 capture confidence + persist a correction/measurement channel → L1+L2 gate-and-read-back as ONE feature (yes/no vocab doesn't exist yet; "again" collides with the #78 menu re-read; confirm hook must sit ABOVE the <3-char gate and busy-park) → L4 one GM DEFAULT_RULES line (unintelligible → ask in-fiction; drift-surface, own commit) → measure → L3 Haiku proofread (~$0.0004/utt, restricted-shortlist only) ONLY if measurements demand it. Typed path confirmed to have zero inspection; the structural garble class ("Shalelu Korunn symbol") is provably beyond token-aligned substitution — L3 territory. <br>🧪 **Ready to test — BUILT v1.548 (Layers 0–2, user go 2026-08-06) — awaiting FIELD validation (the row's own "needs to be played a bit" gate).** L0: `include[]=logprobs` rides every gpt-4o transcription request (whisper-1 fallback runs confidence-less by design), native per-chunk `confidence` read (0 = no-signal, the Edge-Canary class), `sttLogEvent` ring (`tnd_stt_log_v1`, cap 100, counts+reasons never transcripts — read via `sttLogAll()`). L1-gate: `sttCorrectNames` gains a collector (3rd arg, omitted = byte-identical, regression-pinned); corrections feed `sttSuspicion` (helpers.js — reasons: low-confidence <0.66 / far-correction sc≥10 / common-bigram (the "there is"→Daeris FP class) / multiple-corrections / unknown-name; null confidence NEVER flags alone — the native path must not confirm everything). L2: suspicion on the auto-send path → Car Mode speaks "I heard: … — send it?" + spoken **yes/no/redo** (`parseConfirmCommand`, helpers.js — same anchored false-positive discipline as parseCarCommand, battery-tested incl. "no time to lose"/"yes and I draw my sword"; "repeat" re-reads; 2 unrecognized answers fail safe to park-in-field); non-car auto-send holds + amber-flags the field; File ▸ Admin ▸ 🛡 Confirm unclear voice input (default ON); confirm interceptor sits ABOVE carVoiceCommand/busy-park/<3-char gate (the #78 ordering lesson — a spoken "no" is 2 chars); nothing touches sessionLog/transcript until confirmed (pending text lives in `_confirmPending`, field cleared); cloud confirm answers stay push-to-talk (the round-2 #25 hallucination rule — a hallucinated non-answer can't send, parseConfirmCommand refuses it). L3 stays evidence-gated on the sttLogAll record. |

Fixed by extending the existing protection list; name-snapping verified intact. <br><br>**Verified:** 851 green (6 new: speech shape, pick vocabulary, the false-positive battery, repeat-vs-repeatAll, out-of-range, and the name-correction regression) + **live in-browser through the real functions** — 1st `_carAutoMic` speaks the menu / 2nd falls through to the mic; "option two" sends "Ask about the letter"; "I attack the second guard" NOT consumed; hold→poll→read when options arrive late; give-up fallback spoken; "repeat" with no options warns; a consumed command always leaves `#action-input` empty (and while `busy` parks the RESOLVED action text, not the word "three"); zero console errors. **Not covered:** real-device mic accuracy in a moving car (CAR_MODE.md's standing risk, ties to #19). |

| 93 | **B14 splitter edges, post-#96 rescope: an unclosed quote can voice NARRATION as a character (from the entry-4 Fable review, brief F; rescoped 2026-07-27).** TLDR: of the three original edge behaviors, #96's segment-claiming deriver killed one and UPGRADED another. **① THE SURVIVING CORE — unbalanced-quote parity inversion:** a stray unclosed quote flips `splitSentences`' dialogue/narration `spk` labels for the rest of the paragraph, and since v1.451 the consequence is worse than mislabeling: `deriveSpeakerMapFromTags` assigns voices per-unit mechanically, so inverted NARRATION units get the segment's CHARACTER voice — narration read aloud in Ameiko's voice (the old model post-pass usually omitted such lines; the deterministic deriver won't). The safe direction (dialogue→narrator) is unchanged. Candidate fix unchanged: reset `_inDlg` at a paragraph boundary only when quote parity CLOSED at that boundary (the continued-speech convention — unclosed quotes DO continue across paragraphs — must keep working, B14c-tested). **③ minor tail:** a lone `"` fragment can reach synthesis as a junk audio blip (the fold loop's `k > 0` bound skips index-0 punctuation-only pieces) — forward-fold them. **② RETIRED (2026-07-27):** the adjacent-paragraph span MERGE is dead as a live concern — the v1.451 deriver iterates units and never calls `speakerSpans` (grep: zero live callers; engine tests keep it as a harness helper), and merged spans split correctly across `[SAY:]` segments (field-fixture-tested). Low-frequency, no text loss. **①b NEW from the entry-7 Fable review (2026-07-27, brief E): a SECOND cross-assignment class with well-formed quotes** — `_sayNorm` strips quote marks from segment text, so the deriver is quote-blind inside a segment: narration in speaker A's segment that happens to contain the text of speaker B's later tagged line captures B's line into A's voice (probe W1: `Frizwick will only say ten out of ten, as always.` inside Daeris's segment stole Frizwick's `"Ten out of ten,"`). Needs quote-aware matching (require the key's hit to start inside a quoted run of the raw segment) — same session as ①, same test bed. Sharpened ① evidence: a `[SAY:]` tag CONVERTS a parity fault from silent-flat (no tags → map null) into audible wrong-voice, a following tag bounds the over-reach but hands the leftover narration to the WRONG second voice, and a paragraph break confines the damage (`_inQ` resets per paragraph); a stray CLOSING quote (probe Y8) is unsafe the same way. Splitter note: the parity counter counts `["”]` but not `“`, so curly quotes orphan each closer into its own unit — theoretical today (0 curly in 13,505 GM quote chars, brief D census), record only. The id list now DERIVES from SKILLS in tag_table.js (data.js loads first; load-time constant, so the cache byte-identity contract holds), pinned by a bidirectional engine guard (doc ids ⇆ SKILLS, proven red-first on the missing id); frozen doc hash re-baselined (+12 = ", Explosives"). |
| 72c | **#72 COMPLETED RECORD — class bible: origin, spec, editor, authored classes, and the C1–C7 engine wiring.** TLDR: the finished half of #72, moved here 2026-08-03 so the open row stays legible — nothing in this row is open work. <br><br>**ORIGIN + DESIGN (the original row):** **Player levelling has holes — audit and design a real progression, per class.** TLDR: levelling up mostly gives HP and an occasional one-line passive; casters get NO new spells after level 3, two levels give nothing at all, and the XP table dead-ends at 10. **Traced 2026-07-18 from "what happened when Ammut hit level 9?" — the answer was: +HP and one passive (Blindsense), nothing else.** Findings: **①** `checkLevelUp` never touches `character.spells` — spells are granted at creation (capped tier 3 by `SPELL_PICK_LIMITS`) and at the level-3 archetype pick, and never again; `initSpells` only backfills an EMPTY list, so it is not a progression path. A level 9 Arcane Trickster holds the same book they had at level 3 plus GM ad-hoc grants, and `SPELLS` has a Necromancer tier 4 no player can ever reach by progression. **②** The curve is lumpy: L2/5/7/9 = one class feature, L3 = archetype, L4/8 = stat bump, **L6 and L10 = HP only** — and L10, the last level in the game, is one of the empty ones. **③** `getLvl` hard-clamps at 10 (64,000 XP); past that XP accrues and buys nothing (Ammut sits at 51,100 on a t833 campaign, so the ceiling is close). Decide: soft-cap with prestige-style rewards · extend the table · or deliberately end campaigns there. **④** Cosmetic: features are stored `nm:"Lv9"`, so the sheet lists an ability literally named "Lv9" instead of "Blindsense". **THE WORK:** go class by class (9 classes) and design a full 1-10 progression — features, spell/slot growth, and what fills the dead levels — then land it in a **`class_bible.js`**. **Naming checked (user asked whether `class` is a protected word):** `class_bible`/`CLASS_BIBLE` are perfectly legal — only the BARE identifier `class` is reserved, and this codebase already ships `CLASS_FEATURES` as a global. The real risk is CONCEPTUAL collision, not syntax. **SCOPE RULED 2026-07-18 (user): a single definitive source — `CLSS` is MIGRATED THEN DELETED, not kept as an alias** ("leaving both alive is how you get a third source six months from now"). data.js has **seven** class-keyed tables, 52 refs, almost all in char-creation.js + game.js: `CLSS` (30 refs — hd/prime/gear/desc) · `CLASS_FEATURES` (4) · `ABILS` (2, starting abilities) · `ARCHETYPES` (6) · `ARCH_SPELLS` (3) · `STAT_PRIORITY` (3) · `SPELLS` (4). **The bible absorbs all of them EXCEPT `SPELLS`** — spell MECHANICS already have a canonical home in `capability_bible.js`, and folding them in would re-fragment exactly what #10 unified. Instead the class bible lists spell NAMES per tier and the capability bible owns what they do — the pattern `racial_caps` already uses for ancestries. **The class bible holds progression and references, not mechanics.** **SEQUENCING (do not big-bang this — it drives creation AND `checkLevelUp`):** ① introduce **`classDef(id)` as THE lookup first**, collapsing ~30 hand-rolled `for(i…) if(CLSS[i].id===c.cls)` loops (there is no such helper today — the same smell `wsNpcByName` fixed for NPCs) into one function; pure refactor, independently valuable, testable in isolation. ② THEN swap the backing store to `CLASS_BIBLE` — now a one-line change *inside that helper*, because nothing else touches the table directly. ③ Delete `CLSS`. **Invariant test either way: an existing character's derived values must not move** — load a live save before/after and assert identical hit die, features, archetype list, and stat priority; a silent shift there corrupts saves.<br><br>**THE SHIPPING LOG (the original status cell):** Filed 2026-07-18. Design-first: the progression tables are a balance question, not a coding one. ⛨ Touches the level-up write path + the char sheet — engine-test the single-player invariants and existing saves (a mid-campaign character must not retroactively gain or lose anything). **Spec at R2, ALL FORKS RULED (user, 2026-07-27): [DOC/DOC_class_bible.html](DOC/DOC_class_bible.html).** R1 same day carried the measured inventory (85/85 class spells already resolve in the capability bible; archetypes confirmed pure flavor — one `{nm,desc}` ability at L3, nothing after). Rulings: **C2 = PICKS** at tier-unlock levels (reuse the creation picker; full casters T2@5 T3@7 T4@9 T5@11 T6@15, content caps at T6, tier system stays open-ended beyond) · **C4/C4b = EXTEND the table to L20** (the full classic arc, overriding the soft-cap rec; draft XP curve to ~355k@20; `STAT_BUMP_LEVELS`→`[4,8,12,16,19]`) · **C5 = creation-only familiarity seeds** (`skillSeeds`, 2-3 signature skills at Familiar; existing saves untouched). The 1–20 grid: class 2/5/7/9/11/13/15/17 · archetype 3/6/10/14/18 + CAPSTONE@20 · bumps 4/8/12/16/19 — every level grants something. Honest authoring bill at L20 scope: ~250–260 entries (36 class features + 162 archetype rows + ~55 T5/T6 spells), shippable per-class. **Iteration 2 SHIPPED v1.464 (redefined by the user: skeleton + editor first, user fills data):** `class_bible.js` generated from the live engine tables (9 classes · 27 archetypes · 234 level slots, 36 seeded with REAL feature names — the `nm:"Lv9"` bug structurally dead — 198 fill-phase blanks · XP 1–20) + **`bible_editor.html`**, the dev-only authoring satellite (SEPARATE from read-only bible_study by user call — bibles may become player-facing). Editor: per-name resolve badges, fixed-6-attribute capability define form, localStorage draft, exports regenerated file + paste-ready capability additions. Guards: BIBLE EDITOR CONTRACT (serializer↔file byte-pin, sabotage-proven both directions) + coverage guard (every bible spell name must resolve in capability_bible — additions merge in the SAME commit) + 6 structural engine tests (871 green). NOT engine-wired: legacy tables remain truth until C6. **v1.467 — EDITOR v2: open a bible, save back over it** (user: a big spell fill exported to ~/Downloads instead of the project dir). File System Access API — verified live on `file://` — gives a real handle, so **Save overwrites the original in place** behind a confirm; a lapsed handle parks behind a **Reconnect** button re-granted inside a click (the #30 permission lesson); no-FSA browsers get a file input + download fallback, stated loudly. **Bible types are now a REGISTRY** (`BIBLE_TYPES`: detect/parse/serialize/render — the item bible later is ONE entry, not new branches): **class_bible** (regenerated wholesale) + **capability_bible** (hand-commented → untouched entries re-emit as their ORIGINAL SOURCE LINES, comments preserved in place, so a save is a minimal diff — 175 entries, 18 comments, verified). New capability view: filterable list, per-entry edit through the same 6-attribute form, add/delete. Draft shape v2, and **v1 class-only drafts are MIGRATED, never discarded** (a fill session must survive the upgrade). Contract extended and sabotage-proven 4/4 — ⚠ the first version MISSED two mutations because it only exercised the unedited path, leaving `emit()` (the code that writes your edits) untested; an all-dirty round-trip clause now pins it. Live testing also caught the capability view not refreshing the dirty flag / edited count (renderCap bypassed the shell refresh; markDirty now updates both synchronously). 874 green. <br><br>**v1.466 — FIRST ARCHETYPE AUTHORED: Arcane Trickster, end to end** (user approved 2026-07-28: "I like it, might lead to some fun situations"). Six features + six paired capability entries in one commit: **L3 The Old Switcheroo** (Mage Hand swaps two objects, both holders keep believing) · **L6 It Was Their Idea** (planted suggestion remembered as the target's own) · **L10 Seventy-Two Shapes** (take any form you've seen, holds until dropped) · **L14 Never Caught** (first seizure each scene simply doesn't happen) · **L18 The Story Runs Ahead** (a rumour is true by the time it arrives) · **L20 Turnabout** (CAPSTONE — exchange place, bonds, condition and blame with another creature). Spine verb = **the SWAP**: object → idea → face → blame → story → fate. Register is Loki/Anansi/Wukong per the user's redirect ("trickster" = mischief and chaos, NOT the shadow-operative first draft — that material is now earmarked for Thief/Assassin). ⚠ **A rejected capstone produced the enforceability ceiling** (below): the first draft erased the character from a past event, which the user flagged as dangerous to RAG — correctly. This is the **TEMPLATE** the other 26 archetypes follow; 42/234 slots filled. <br><br>**2026-07-28 — the ENFORCEABILITY CEILING, the second authoring test (spec Part II-a):** a feature's blast radius must not exceed the engine's vocabulary for enforcing it. **Generative is cheap, subtractive is expensive** — creating facts is native; unwriting them has almost no vocabulary (`worldState.transcript` is immutable by decree, `[RETCON:]` de-indexes exactly TWO entries, and an event also lives in chapters/eventHistory/lore/keyDecisions/npcs.knowledge/npcs.events/npcGraph/quests/storyBeats/coreMemories-across-every-sheet). RAG would re-serve the original scene non-deterministically → silent drift, reading as a broken ability. Survivable form: scope "they forget" to what people KNOW (current-state blocks outrank RAG excerpts by design), never to the record. Literal event erasure = a real `[EVENT_ERASE:]` feature (turn-RANGE rc mark + witness fan-out + summary-tier scrub), Fable-tier, its own session — **not filed as a row, deliberately; raise it if the fiction ever demands it.** Both this and the narratability test render in the editor's Archetypes header. <br><br>**2026-07-28 — the CONCEPT behind the rubric (spec Part II-a):** class answers "what can you do", archetype answers "who are you when you do it" — class levels are training, archetype levels are *becoming*. Load-bearing for THIS engine: features are injected as canon every turn, so an archetype level is **a sentence the GM permanently knows about the character**, not a sheet number — therefore features must be **narratable, not numeric** ("+2 to hit" is invisible to an LLM GM; "you can always tell when someone is lying to you about money" gets reached for unprompted). Placement test = **blast radius**: yourself (L3–6) → the scene (L10–14) → the world (L18) → the story (L20). Both the narratability test and the radius ladder now render in the editor's Archetypes header, where the 162 slots actually get filled. <br><br>**v1.465 — archetype levels DEFINED (user: "define archetype levels for me"):** ① the **rubric** — each of the six archetype rows has a stated job (L3 IDENTITY · L6 REFINEMENT · L10 REACH · L14 MASTERY · L18 APEX · L20 CAPSTONE), shown in the editor at the point of authoring so 162 slots aren't 162 arbitrary calls (class rows deliberately have none — the class is the chassis, the archetype is the character); ② **C7, the third-caster gap CLOSED** — Arcane Trickster + Eldritch Knight had spell benches but NO unlock schedule (spellTiers was generated only for the 6 full/half-caster classes), so AT's T4/Oubliate was unreachable under the C2 picks ruling. Tiers now key to the archetype's own rows: **T1@3 · T2@10 · T3@14 · T4@18**; EK gains blank T3/T4 for the fill phase. Editor gained per-archetype tier controls (the schedule was un-tunable before) + the rubric line. Engine-tested (872 green): both caster archetypes carry a schedule, every unlock level must BE one of that archetype's rows, every unlockable tier has a spells array. **v1.472 — C6 ① SHIPPED: classDef()/classDefs() are THE class lookup.** TLDR: every hand-rolled CLSS scan in the engine now routes through two helpers, so the ② store swap is an edit inside them and nowhere else. helpers.js gains classDefs() (the list) + classDef(id) (exact canonical-id match first, then a trimmed case-insensitive fallback — the normalizeCompanionSheet model-output path, byte-identical behavior there; null on miss so every caller keeps its own fallback: getMHP 8, companionBaselineHp hd 10, unknown-class hpGain 3). Rewired: checkLevelUp, checkCompanionLevelUp, buildCompanionSheetPrompt, companionBaselineHp, normalizeCompanionSheet (game.js) · buildClsGrid, pickCls + gear preview, both cls/anc scans, the AI-assist ctx (char-creation.js) · getMHP (helpers.js). Grep proves CLSS is read ONLY inside classDefs(). 5 new engine tests written FIRST (879 green): classDefs↔CLSS identity, all-9 exact resolve to the same objects, CI+trim fallback, the null contract, and the normalizeCompanionSheet canonicalization pinned before the change. Wizard verified live in the browser (9-cell grid, pick → selection + gear preview, zero console errors). **v1.474 — SECOND ARCHETYPE AUTHORED: the Assassin, end to end** (user-directed slate 2026-07-28 — L6/L10 user-authored, L14/L18 picked from candidates, ledger framing rejected and memorialized). Spine verb = **the ENDING**: **L3 The First Blow Is the Last** (strike an unsuspecting target — auto-crit, fight often over before it begins) · **L6 Master Poisoner** (any food/weapon lethally poisoned instantly, creatively) · **L10 Sure Shot** (1/combat, an impossible ranged shot hits critical — ricochets, bank shots, needle-threading; if you can see it you can kill it) · **L14 A Natural Death** (kills wear whatever face you choose; shapes what the world BELIEVES, never the record — the ceiling-survivable form) · **L18 Angel of Death** (per-day, nothing bars your approach to a target **within 150ft** — the user's end-run guard baked into the range attribute and pinned by test) · **L20 The Inevitable End** (CAPSTONE, 1/campaign: name a target, their death becomes when-not-if; the world supplies opportunity, never the kill). All six MUNDANE (isMagical:false, category martial — no caster tradition draws them; an enemy martial could). Six paired capability entries + six class rows in ONE commit per the coverage discipline; red-first template test (six rows, all resolve, all martial, capstone + 150ft pinned); 885 green; editor live-verified (Rogue view, six green resolve badges). **52/234 slots filled.** The recovered shadow-operative material (Never the Suspect, Memory Thief) was ultimately CUT here too (L10 went to Sure Shot, L14 not-death-oriented) — it remains available for the Thief, whose slate is drafted and awaiting the user's notes. **v1.475 — THIRD ARCHETYPE: the Thief (slate approved as drafted 2026-07-28) — ROGUE COMPLETE, all three archetypes authored.** Spine verb = **the TAKE** (object → lock → place → person → world → the impossible): **L3 Legerdemain** · **L6 No Lock Holds** · **L10 The Casing Eye** · **L14 The Shirt Off Your Back** · **L18 Word of the Score** (passive — rumor of every score finds you) · **L20 The Impossible Theft** (CAPSTONE, 1/campaign: steal a god's flame, a house's luck — GENERATIVE by construction, 'creates a possession, never unwrites the past', the ceiling clause test-pinned in the effect text). All martial/non-magical like the Assassin. 886 green; editor live-verified. **58/234 slots filled.** The recovered Never the Suspect / Memory Thief material is now formally UNUSED — three Rogue archetypes shipped without it; keep or retire at will. **v1.476 — WARRIOR ⅔ AUTHORED: Champion + Battle Master** (slates approved 2026-07-28; A Call to Arms user-authored). **Champion**, spine = **the FEAT** (Achilles/Heracles, mundane): L3 The Telling Blow · L6 Peak of the Body · L10 Last One Standing · L14 One Against Many · L18 Strength of Legend · L20 The Challenge (CAPSTONE 1/campaign — call any creature by name to single combat; the circle admits no outside power). **Battle Master**, spine = **the READ**: L3 The Read · L6 Wordless Command · L10 The Turning Move (1/combat) · L14 The Battlefield Is a Board · L18 **A Call to Arms** (conscript **5d10 locals, each level 2d6**, armed to station — **one army at a time, raising anew disbands the old**, the anti-fishing limit; dice + limit clause test-pinned) · L20 The Plan Holds (CAPSTONE 1/campaign — steps succeed as described; 'the world may complicate the aftermath, never the steps' test-pinned; NOTE: the first capstone draft was a retroactive this-was-the-plan-all-along reveal, self-rejected as a ceiling violation — retroactive fact-creation about the past is the event-erasure class). All 12 mundane martial. 887 green; editor live-verified. **70/234 slots filled.** **v1.477 — the Eldritch Knight: WARRIOR COMPLETE (2nd full class).** Spine = **the BOND** (blade and spell one thing), and unlike its siblings it is **MAGICAL/arcane** — an enemy arcane caster can draw these (the category gate). L3 Weapon Bond · L6 Spellblade · **L10 Sundering Stroke** · **L14 Soul Steal** · L18 The Standing Ward · L20 The Blade Keeps Faith (CAPSTONE — death refuses the bond; 'what happened still happened, you simply are not dead of it', ceiling-clean). **Two user calls shaped it:** ① **Soul Steal** replaced the drafted Warded Edge — a Stormbringer beat, user-authored: the blade drinks the soul of what it cuts, taking one ability for **4 hours**, **three held at once**, nothing new taken while full (a TRANSFER — the victim lacks it while you hold it; present-state, ceiling-clean, and the 4h window rides the #73 clock). All three limits ride in the injected canon and are test-pinned, because the limits ARE the ability's shape. ② **L10/L14 SWAPPED** (user: 'seem like they should switch places' — agreed: cut magic at REACH, drink beings at MASTERY; it also moves the strongest non-capstone feature in the book off L10 to sit with its true peers). Order test-pinned so it can't drift back. Also added: a **half-authored guard** — every archetype must have 6 rows filled or 0, never in between (a partial archetype reads to the GM as a character with gaps). 889 green; editor live-verified (six green badges, swapped order confirmed in the DOM). **76/234 slots filled — Rogue and Warrior both COMPLETE.** **v1.482 — the WARRIOR CLASS LADDER authored to a user-specified slate** (2026-07-28; the class rows, distinct from the archetype spines). L2 Action Surge (unchanged, "good as is") · **L5 Indomitable** (moved L7→L5) · **L7 Resilience** (recover 1 HP per level per hour — a Warrior who walks off a battlefield is whole by nightfall, no healer) · **L9 Taunt** (foes attack YOU, not your companions) · **L11 Double Attack** · **L13 Counter Attack** (d20 per incoming melee attack; under your level it is countered outright and you answer). Two deliberate displacements: **Extra Attack and Indomitable(x2) LEAVE the ladder** (the attack upgrade returns at L11 as Double Attack). L15/L17 stay blank — not specified; a blank is a fill-phase slot, not an error. All six are mundane martial, and all six **close six of the ~30 seeded class features that carried prose but no injectable canon** — the GM now gets real mechanics, not a one-line label. ⚠ Two judgment calls flagged rather than buried: **Taunt** had no stated duration/limit and an unbounded forced-target is unenforceable, so it ships 1/combat · 3 rounds · WIS save for named foes (a test pins that it stays bounded); **Counter Attack** is implemented exactly as specified including its scaling — **60% of incoming melee countered at L13, 95% at L20**, very strong and auto-growing, flagged for a later balance pass if it plays hot. 895 green. The Lv9-naming canary legitimately broke (its fixture WAS Warrior L9) — repointed to Primal L9, a class not yet re-authored; the general no-Lv<N> scan that is the actual guard is untouched. Live-verified in the editor. **78/234 slots filled.** ⚠ NOT live in game: class_bible is not engine-wired until C6-②, so data.js CLASS_FEATURES remains truth for play — expected fill-phase divergence, documented in the file header. **v1.483 — Warrior L15 + the Counter Attack rebalance** (user call same day, on the flagged scaling). **Counter Attack now halves damage instead of negating it**, still buying the free answering strike — a TRADE (eat part of the blow to open the attacker up) rather than the flat immunity it became at high level, where the counter fires on 95% of incoming melee at L20. User's own framing: "that way a warrior can open up a target to sacrifice a little damage to deal a lot." The trade rides in the effect text so the GM narrates it as a choice; the test pins BOTH halves (roll-under-level AND damage halved, not negated). **L15 Iron Constitution** (user-specified): every CON save auto-succeeds except on a natural 1 — **the exception IS the feature** (an auto-pass with no failure case gives the GM nothing to narrate), test-pinned in the injected canon. 895 green, editor-verified. **79/234 slots.** **L17 remains OPEN** — the last Warrior class row, pending the user's pick from the offered options (Unstoppable / Triple Attack / Killing Ground). **v1.484 — WARRIOR COMPLETE: L5 Stunning Blow + L17 Unstoppable** (user picks, same session). **L17 Unstoppable** (chosen from three offered): while you still have hit points nothing takes you out of the fight — no stun, paralysis, restraint, prone, disarm, grapple, or forced movement, mundane or magical. It finishes the sentence Iron Constitution starts (L15: the body can't be broken down · L17: it can't be stopped) — *"You can be killed. You cannot be handled."* The **while-you-have-HP bound is test-pinned**; without it the immunity would absurdly persist while downed. **L5 Stunning Blow replaces Indomitable** (user: "what I don't really love is Indomitable"): forgo a hit's damage to stun instead, CON save negates, and **any creature can be caught by it only ONCE** — a foe who has felt it never falls for the same blow twice. That per-target limit reads as character rather than bookkeeping and makes the feature a decision, not a rotation; all three parts (trade · save · once-per-target) are test-pinned because all three ARE the shape of the feature. Nice emergent rhyme: the Warrior learns to stun at L5 and becomes unstunnable at L17. **Indomitable stays IN capability_bible** though it left the ladder — legacy CLASS_FEATURES still grants it at L7 in live play until C6-②, and the coverage guard only runs one way (a bible NAME must resolve; not every entry must be used). **WARRIOR is the first class complete in both halves** — all 8 class rows (2/5/7/9/11/13/15/17) AND all three archetypes (Champion · Battle Master · Eldritch Knight). 895 green, editor-verified, **80/234 slots filled**. **Next: the remaining 7 classes (Sorcerer/Ranger/Primal/Paladin/Cleric/Druid/Necromancer — Rogue's class rows are already 8/8 from the earlier seed, its archetypes done); then C6 ②③ store swap + CLSS delete (Fable, save-invariant tested).** **2026-07-31 — CLERIC ROUGHED OUT (draft, awaiting the user's review).** TLDR: all 22 blank Cleric slots are filled with narratable prose and the four seeded class rows re-authored, so the class reads end to end — but **no paired capability entries yet**, deliberately: the names may move under review, and the coverage discipline says they land in the approval commit. Class ladder (chassis = divine authority): L2 Channel Divinity (*the god answers the priest, not the request*) · L5 Destroy Undead · L7 Divine Strike · L9 Divine Intervention (d100 under level; answered or not, silent for seven days) · **L11 Consecrated Ground** (one at a time, the Call-to-Arms limit shape) · **L13 The Blessing Holds** (boons run to your next rest, not to the end of the fight) · **L15 Death Waits** (1/rest, and never the same creature twice in a day) · **L17 Smite** (user-authored 2026-07-31, replacing the moved The Word: one blow equal to 95% of the target's remaining hit points, delivered in the deity's own idiom — thunderclap, ghostly kraken tentacles — GM's choice of manifestation. Two bounds added by Opus and flagged: **1/day**, and **95% of REMAINING hp — never a killing blow**, so the god brings a foe to death's door and the kill still belongs to the party). Domains, each with a spine verb: **Life = the MENDING** (domain review 2026-07-31, user rulings applied: Disciple of Life · **Cleanse** (up from L10) · **Preserve Life** (down to L10) · **Restore Limb** at L14 (new, replacing The Wound Does Not Take — regrows anything short of a head) · **Battlefield Miracle** at L18 (renamed from No One Dies Today, restricted to ALLIES — enemies enjoy no such protection) · **The Return**, capstone 1/campaign, **cost clause removed** (user: it threw the capstone out of whack with its peers)) · **War = the FIGHT — user-approved as drafted** (War Priest · Guided Strike · The Line Holds · Consecrated Arms · The God of Battle Attends · **Decide the Battle**) · **SUBJUGATION = the YOKE** (review applied: **The Yoke** up to L6, **The Knee** down to L10; L14 renamed **The Missive**) (user ruling 2026-07-31: the drafted Trickery Domain duplicated the Arcane Trickster's spine — 4 of 6 rows on the same beats — so it was cut same-day and replaced; heal / kill / OWN, three verbs with no shared surface): **The Knee** (your voice presses wills — never puppets bodies) · **The Oath** (willingly-spoken promises bind) · **The Yoke** (bind the defeated into awake, resentful service, one at a time) · **Nothing Refuses You** (compulsion without presence, one standing command abroad) · **The Word** (moved from class L17 — it IS the domain identity; one command in the god's voice, everyone who hears obeys) · **The Law** (capstone 1/campaign — one decree the land itself enforces until a god's own authority overturns it). **Necromancer boundary, ruled by the user:** Possess Thrall is full possession (the necromancer INSIDE the thrall, its eyes and mouth); Subjugation only SUPERSEDES the target's will — awake, aware, no connection between cleric and target. Baked into the L10 prose so the two never converge. The rename shipped as the v1.506 id-alignment commit (trickery→subjugation rode `ARCHETYPE_ID_RENAMES`). **2026-07-31 — SORCERER ROUGHED OUT (draft, same review-first shape as the Cleric: no capability entries until approval).** User slate: **Bayaz / Elric / Raistlin / Doctor Strange — magic has a price and the body is the ledger.** Class ladder 8/8 (seeded rows re-authored + 4 new): L2 Font of Magic · L5 Metamagic · L7 Sorcerous Restoration · L9 Overchannel (max effect, d6/tier self-damage unhealable until sleep) · **L11 Arcane Sight** · **L13 Split Weave** (two concentrations) · **L15 Body of Magic** · **L17 Witnessed Once, Worked Once** (1/day cast any spell ever seen cast). Domains-of-the-arcane, spine verbs: **Draconic Bloodline = the BLOOD** (Old Blood · Elemental Vein · Presence of the Wyrm · Wings · Wyrmform 1/day · **The Progenitor's Due** — capstone 1/campaign, the ancestor dragon answers in the flesh) · **Wild Magic = the SURGE** (Tides of Chaos · Bend Luck · Controlled Chaos — the surge offers two, you pick · Chaos Feeds You · Unraveling Presence · **Rewrite the Odds** — capstone, declare one chance outcome) · **Shadow Magic = the DARK** (Eyes of the Dark · Hound of Ill Omen · Shadow Walk · The Shadow Half · The Dark Between · **Sovereign of the Night** — capstone, dusk-to-dawn the night is yours). skillSeeds proposed Arcana + Lore. T4–6 spell benches stay blank (no arcane entries at those tiers yet — new authoring). **124/234 slots.** Spell bench gained only names that already resolve: T1 Command/Sanctuary/Detect Magic · T2 Augury/Zone of Truth · T4 Divination; **T5/T6 stay blank — no divine capability entry exists at those tiers yet** (they are new authoring, not a fill). skillSeeds proposed as Lore + First Aid. 939 green; the file was re-serialized through the editor's own serializer, so the BIBLE EDITOR CONTRACT byte-pin holds. **102/234 slots.** <br><br>**C6 COMPLETE (2026-08-03, v1.533 ② + v1.534 ③).** The bible IS the engine store: classDefs()/classDef() swapped to CLASS_BIBLE (castStat ported first — 8 classes, canonical re-serialize), every sibling-table consumer routed through the accessor family (classFeaturesAt/archFeaturesAt/classXpLevels), level-ups grant NAMED class + archetype rows for player and companions, getLvl's hidden 10-cap removed (L11 gate 85000, L20=355000), class_bible.js joined index.html + the SW app shell and left the network-first regex, and the six legacy tables (CLSS/ABILS/ARCHETYPES/CLASS_FEATURES/XP_LEVELS/STAT_PRIORITY) are DELETED from data.js. The spec's invariant is a frozen-literal engine test (hd/prime/castStat/statPriority captured from the live legacy tables minutes before the swap + XP 1-10 verbatim) and was proven against the live t1385 save: Ammut keeps level/hd/mana, next gate 85000, no retroactive grants. <br><br>**C2 COMPLETE (2026-08-03, v1.535).** Spell growth per the ruled spec: spellUnlocksCrossed() (pure, helpers.js) scans class + committed-archetype spellTiers over the crossed interval (half-open — no retroactive grants ever); the player gets a forced-choice tier picker (showSpellUnlockModal, stat-bump house style) queued after archetype/bumps and re-surfaced before the next turn; companions auto-pick silently; C7 third casters (AT/EK) key to the archetype schedule incl. catch-up at the pick; fill-phase blank benches (EK T3/T4) skip loudly; SPELL_UNLOCK_PICKS is the template-iteration count constant. Real-browser proof: Cleric 10→11 raised the actual modal, empty-confirm warned, over-select refused, Flame Strike landed as T5 and manaMax derived 6 (#110 interplay). 1008 green. Ammut's frozen-spells era ends at 85,000 XP. Remaining: fill-phase slots, half-caster tier drafts, per-class pick counts. | — | — | Record row (2026-08-03). Open work lives in #72. |
| 134 | **Missing-interior-description nudge — lived-in rooms get their canon pinned.** TLDR: the SECONDARY hardening from the t1431 multiplying-beds finding (sibling of #133, which carries the primary stale-splitLoc mechanism; full forensics in [Fable analysis](DOC/t1431_drift_analysis_Fable.html) + [Sol analysis](DOC/t1431_drift_analysis_Sol.html)): interior detail has exactly ONE pin, the write-once `[LOCATION_DESC:]`, and the GM filed it for 4/50 (8%) of the live save's sub-locations — an undescribed room's furniture lives only in prose, evaporates within ~2 summarize cycles, and gets re-imagined from genre priors on return. Fix: `buildLocationDescNudge` (api.js, NOTE_BUILDERS) — once the party is SETTLED at a node with `description=null` (past the arrival turn, deliberately: arrival responses are crowded and write-once means a rushed description is pinned forever), an engine note demands `[LOCATION_DESC:]` with PHYSICAL facts and countable furnishings ("facts, not mood"). Latch stamps on fire (`worldState.locDescNudged`); re-fires every `LOC_DESC_NUDGE_COOLDOWN`=10 turns while still null (#29 rot lesson); a filed description ends it permanently; silent in combat; fires only where the party actually dwells. ⚠ Drift surface (engine-note channel) — Fable, test-first. Ship-time confidence, recorded honestly: HIGH that descriptions get filed (the #20/#129 channel is field-proven), MODERATE that furniture drift fully stops (depends on the GM recording countable facts; and per #133 the t1431 incident had a stronger co-cause this nudge does not touch). | S | Fable | ✅ **v1.541**, 2026-08-04. Test-first: 3 engine tests failing → green (1023 total). Field verification: next lived-in undescribed room produces a description within ~2 turns of settling. |

| 132 | **Output-truncation detection — a response cut mid-tag must be loud, not a silent mutation loss plus raw text on screen.** TLDR: the engine has ZERO truncation detection — no provider adapter reads `stop_reason`/`finish_reason` — so when the 1000-token output cap cuts a GM response mid-tag, the trailing fragment renders RAW to the player (`cleanTxt`'s strip regexes require the closing `]`), the mutation is silently lost (all TAG_TABLE handlers match complete tags), and no warn fires anywhere (`__tagUnknownScan` needs a colon). Field case: Runelords t1410 (B21 evidence) ended `…was listening. [SCH` — the GM's demanded `[SCHEDULE_RESOLVED:…]` was cut and lost; harmless there only because the sweep retired the entry anyway, but a truncated `[QUEST:x\|completed]` would silently drop the completion AND leak raw text. Direction (B21 findings sketch ③): each provider's `parseResponse` also surfaces its finish reason; on length-truncation `callGM`/the commit path warns loudly (console + toast + #16 crumb) and strips the trailing unterminated `\[[A-Z_]*:?[^\]]*$` fragment from the DISPLAYED text only (transcript keeps the raw truth). ⚠ Drift surface (cleanTxt + frozen strip hashes, provider adapters, response-commit path) — Fable tier, design review before code; a greedy trailing-fragment strip could eat legitimate bracketed prose, so the regex must anchor to end-of-response. | S | Fable | ✅ **v1.538**, 2026-08-03. `parseFinish` on all 5 provider adapters (OpenAI-shaped share `OPENAI_FINISH`); callGM warns loud (console + toast + #16 crumb) on a length-cap finish; cleanTxt drops a trailing unterminated ≥3-caps tag fragment (end-anchored; complete tags and lowercase bracket prose untouched; sessionLog keeps the raw). Test-first: 2 tests failing → green (1016). |

Unmappable free text stays flavor (stored, clock untouched). Loud muts line on every reconcile. NOT taken (recorded): deriving/retiring `world.time` injection (GM keeps its narrative channel); editing the `[TIME:]` STATE-TAGS doc line (hash-pinned, behavior is engine-side); Sync-modal manual time edits still don't touch the clock (deliberate override). The field save's standing "dawn"-vs-3:15pm desync heals on the GM's next mappable `[TIME:]` write. Test-first: 4 engine tests incl. the verbatim field numbers (clock 6315 + `[TIME:dawn]` → 7200) → green, 1020 total. |

| 113 | **Whisper STT exploration — where should speech-to-text transcription actually run?** TLDR: today's STT is two paths (stt.js): native Web Speech API where it exists (Chrome/Edge desktop + Android; NOT Firefox, NOT iOS Safari), and a cloud fallback that records the utterance and sends it to OpenAI `whisper-1` **on the player's own OpenAI key** — the path that makes iPhone Safari / Car Mode work at all, and the key-ownership property that was decisive in declining LiveKit. Exploration (findings doc first, NO code): ① **Local WASM Whisper** (whisper.cpp / transformers.js) — the Piper #41 pattern: vendored, offline, $0, model cached in OPFS; questions: tiny/base model size (~40-150MB) vs fantasy-noun accuracy, cold-start + per-utterance latency on a phone, and WASM memory pressure on iOS Safari (the r8/jetsam monotonic-resources lesson — soak test, not one input). ② **Self-hosted Whisper on the tnd-tts Fly app** — the #90 M1 pattern: same warm-daemon/volume machinery, auth via the game session token, removes the OpenAI-key requirement for connected players; questions: per-utterance round-trip vs the 15s MediaRecorder cap, cost on our box, and whether the STT ladder should mirror `TTS_LADDER` (native → server → cloud-on-player-key → none). ③ **Status quo** as the control. Decision criteria: the iOS Safari / Car Mode path is the critical consumer (#78 Phase 2 wants spoken 1/2/3 action picking), accuracy on fantasy proper nouns (measure with and without the `sttCorrectNames` backstop — relates #77), and who pays. Output: a DOC/ findings page + a go/no-go per option; any build work becomes its own row. | M | Opus | ✅ **Exploration complete 2026-08-03** — findings: [DOC/DOC_whisper_stt.html](DOC/DOC_whisper_stt.html). Verdicts: local WASM **NO-GO** (least accuracy, worst latency, second iOS wasm memory ratchet — the B9 class); self-hosted on Fly **DEFER** to the subscription proxy era; status quo + four upgrades **GO** (send Whisper's prompt-bias param with roster/location names — we never have; model swap whisper-1 → gpt-4o-mini-transcribe with fallback; WebAudio silence endpointing replacing the 15s wall; mic-path constraints audit + narrowband-BT telemetry into #16 crumbs). Key car finding: the dominant factor is likely the Bluetooth hands-free mic (telephone-band capture) — engine-independent, measure-then-mitigate. **§4 upgrades SHIPPED v1.536 (user go 2026-08-03):** prompt-bias on every request (sttBiasPrompt, helpers — engine-tested; party/roster/places/quests, deduped, 800-char cap at a name boundary), gpt-4o-mini-transcribe primary with a LOUD whisper-1 retry + #16 crumb, WebAudio silence endpointing (1.5s quiet after speech ends the take; 45s hard cap replaces the 15s wall; noisy-cabin floor degrades to cap-only, never a cut-off; singleton ctx per the monotonic-resources rule), and mic getSettings telemetry (stt-mic crumb + one-per-session narrowband toast in Car Mode). 5-clause STT UPGRADES CONTRACT sabotage-proven 5/5. Honest limit: the mic itself can't be harness-driven — the next real car session is the field test; watch the stt-mic crumbs' sampleRate. Local-WASM/self-host verdicts stand as before. |
| 110 | **Mana pool — replace one-cast-per-spell with a spend-by-tier casting economy.** TLDR: today every known spell is castable exactly once per rest (`used:true`), so a L15 Cleric can heal the party once a day; a mana pool makes casting a resource decision — spam Healing Word or spend big on Spirit Guardians. **Design ruled with the user 2026-07-31 (grew out of the Cleric review):** ① **base mana = the SUM of the known bench's tiers** — self-balancing, scales automatically with picks and tier unlocks, no separate curve table; a player who wants the old behavior can still cast each spell once, but mixing is now legal. ② **Cantrips cost 0** — tier 0 stays at-will, exactly what `cost:"at-will"` already means in the capability bible. ③ **Stat modifier keyed to each class's PRIME CASTING STAT** (a data field per class, not per tradition; | 106 | **In-game time runs far too slowly, and the clock silently loses most of what the narration describes.** TLDR: 216 turns of Day 1 play advanced the campaign clock 1043 minutes while the narration actually described ~2332 — the clock is running at under half the story's pace, and a day takes ~200 turns to pass. **Measured 2026-07-30** from the live save (`Ammut_t1265.tnd`, Rise of the Runelords) — full line-item evidence in [timeline_day1.html](timeline_day1.html) (216 rows, per-turn duration reconstructed from the narration under the GM's own reference scale, then compared against the real stored clock). ⚠ Day 1 of this campaign is **turns 1050–1265, not the campaign's first day** — the clock shipped at v1.389 (≈t917) and `migrateWorldState` starts an old save's clock at Day 0 on load ([state.js:254](state.js:254)), so the counter began mid-campaign; the t1049 `[REST:long]` rolled it to the 1440 dawn boundary that opens Day 1. **TWO INDEPENDENT CAUSES, both confirmed:** ① **the reference scale prices actions, not scenes** — [tag_table.js:68](tag_table.js:68) gives the GM "conversation 1-5 min", and conversation is **100 of 216 turns** (46% of play), so ~4 min/turn means a 16-hour waking day needs ~200 turns to fill. The scale times the words spoken; the walk over, the waiting, and the aftermath are free. ② **a turn with no `[TIME_ADVANCE:]` advances ZERO minutes, silently** — the handler opens `if(!ts.length)return;` with no warn and no floor, and `clockAdvance` has exactly one caller, so nothing backstops it. The visible symptom in the live save: the clock reads Day 1 17h23m (≈11pm) while `world.time` says "early morning" and the t1263 prose has "Sandpoint waking up around you both" — **the story slept and the clock did not**; the 5 turns classed as narrative time-skips alone carry 555 narrated minutes the clock had no way to receive. **② is the bigger and cheaper lever, and it is a no-silent-failures violation.** Candidate remedies (none ratified): a loud warn (and/or a minimum-1-minute floor) when a story turn advances nothing; a scene-level framing in the reference text ("charge the whole scene, including approach and aftermath"); rescaled category values; an explicit narrative time-skip tag for between-scene gaps. **⚠ Drift surface — the reference scale lives in the STATE TAGS doc block, byte-frozen by the suite (strip/doc hashes), and the tag vocabulary + prompt docs are named surface. Time of day is the projection already fixed by #89 and written into clock.js's header — `(clock%1440)+6h`, dawn=6am — computed every render, never stored, so it cannot desync from the counter; past midnight the calendar hour wraps while the adventuring Day does not (dawn-to-dawn). Transcript entries gained absolute **`.ck`** so a rebuilt past turn shows the time it actually happened (distinct from `.ta` on purpose: `.ta` is what a turn CHARGED and is not derivable across gaps/imports, `.ck` is WHEN it was); entries older than the field render the bare turn number rather than a guess — verified against that failure condition, not just the happy path.
