| 41 | **Re-evaluate TTS providers — hosted (Inworld / Cartesia) + local (Kokoro desktop; Piper / Kitten phone) — pricing is just better.** Inworld's voice/TTS pricing beats Cartesia's per-*character* billing, which is the documented TTS cost pain (see #12: shorter prose to cut the Cartesia bill; a `402` = character-quota exhausted). Swap surface is small and already abstracted: `tts.js` routes Cartesia ↔ native browser voice through one path (`speak()`, key in `KEY_K`, native fallback + red-error indicator per #19), so adding a provider is another adapter in the same shape, not a rewrite. **Three contenders, two categories:** ① **Cartesia** + ② **Inworld** are hosted APIs (keyed, per-character billing, ongoing cost, network latency) — the straight cost swap; ③ **Kokoro** is a different animal — open-weight (~82M param, Apache-licensed) local/self-hosted TTS at **$0/character marginal cost**. Kokoro trades the billing line for a hosting + latency + quality tradeoff and **does NOT fit the "route TTS through the T&D server as a keyed proxy under the subscription model" shape** — it'd be a self-host/on-device adapter, not a proxied-key one, so weigh it as an infra decision, not just a price line. **When building:** ① confirm CURRENT Inworld + Cartesia TTS pricing + free tiers (pricing moves — verify at build time); ② A/B voice quality/latency across all three on real narration (cost only wins if the voice holds up for car-audio, the primary use case — Kokoro especially must clear that bar to justify the self-host burden); ③ mirror the existing fallback discipline (key-missing / request-failed → native voice + reason indicator); ④ keep it provider-agnostic like the LLM `PROVIDERS` table so TTS providers become a swappable list, not a hardcode. Ties to #9 (per-character voices) and #12 (TTS cost); the hosted options fold into the server-side-keys move (Architecture: all TTS calls move behind the T&D server under the subscription model), Kokoro would sit outside it. **User note (2026-07-06):** "the pricing is just better" — the motivation is cost; scope is TTS. **(2026-07-13):** Kokoro added as the self-hosted/zero-marginal-cost third contender. **⚠ RESEARCH FINDING (2026-07-13) — Kokoro is a DESKTOP-local model, not a phone-local one; likely disqualified for the car-audio primary use case.** Speed is a non-issue (Kokoro-82M via WebGPU runs faster than real-time everywhere: ~6.5× RTX 4070, ~3.2× M3 Pro, ~2.4× M2 Air; the "brutal slow" demo-site test was choked shared infra, NOT the model). The blocker is **mobile reliability, not raw speed**: benchmarkers who tested it *explicitly avoid WebGPU on mobile* — mobile Safari/Chrome aggressively reclaim tab memory and WebGPU TTS "**fails ~30% of the time**" on phones (fatal for a long, continuous car session). Compounding: WebGPU mobile coverage is fragmented in 2026 (iOS Safari solid only from **iOS 26**; Android Chrome needs **v121+/Android 12+ with Qualcomm-ARM GPU**) AND **iOS WKWebView / Android WebView don't ship WebGPU by default** — so an installed PWA (Add-to-Home-Screen, exactly our car case) may not even expose it. Sustained thermal throttling is a secondary concern behind the memory-reclamation failure. **Consequence for #41:** the "local/$0-per-char" slot is real but Kokoro doesn't fill it *on a phone*. If phone-local TTS is genuinely wanted, the mobile-first models are **Piper** (75MB, CPU/WASM ~3–5× real-time, NO WebGPU dependency — the robust path, lower voice quality) or **Kitten** (24MB/15M params, purpose-built for mobile browsers, ~2–3× WebGPU / ~1.5–2× WASM). Recommend: keep Kokoro as the *desktop*-local option, treat Inworld/Cartesia as the phone/car path, and if local-on-phone matters, evaluate Piper/Kitten instead of Kokoro. Sources archived in chat 2026-07-13 (offlinetts.com browser-TTS showdown, quick-tts.com WebGPU benchmarks, web.dev WebGPU-in-major-browsers). **(2026-07-13, user call) Piper + Kitten are now explicit evaluation contenders — the local-on-phone candidates to actually try:** • **Piper** — 75MB, CPU/WASM only (no WebGPU fragility → the robust phone path), ~3–5× real-time even on WASM, established (Home Assistant / Raspberry Pi lineage); tradeoff is lower/robotic-er voice quality — must clear the car-audio quality bar. • **Kitten** — 24MB / 15M params, purpose-built for mobile browsers & tight memory, ~2–3× WebGPU / ~1.5–2× WASM; smallest footprint, quality vs Piper TBD. **Eval plan when building:** A/B both on real narration prose *on an actual phone* (not desktop — the whole point is mobile viability), scoring (a) does it stay real-time without stutter through a long session, (b) voice quality good enough for immersive narration, (c) reliability across iOS Safari + Android Chrome + installed-PWA context. Winner (if any) becomes the `$0/char` local-on-phone adapter alongside the hosted Inworld/Cartesia path; both slots coexist behind the provider-agnostic TTS table. **⚠ PIPER FINDING (2026-07-13, first hands-on) — raw Piper "completely ignores punctuation" (flat run-on delivery). Root cause + fix are known and on OUR side, do NOT disqualify Piper on this:** Piper synthesizes each input string as ONE utterance and does not self-insert pauses at sentence boundaries inside a block; the demo pages feed it a whole paragraph at once → run-on. The natural sentence pause in real Piper setups comes from **splitting into sentences + inserting silence** (what the CLI `--sentence-silence` does under the hood), NOT from the model reading the period. **Fix = a shared TTS text-prep pass (build once, helps EVERY provider — provider-agnostic, lives beside the adapter table, not welded to Piper):** ① **sentence-split** the cleaned GM prose, synth each sentence, concatenate with a configurable gap (~200ms mid-paragraph, longer at paragraph breaks); ② **punctuation normalization** — EXTEND the existing native-path pass (v1.76 already does em/en-dash → "..." for a spoken pause per #9) to ellipses/semicolons/dashes → clean pause cues; ③ **trim trailing punctuation** before synth (documented Piper gotcha: text ending in punctuation can emit a burst of static at the tail). So the honest eval note: judge Piper's *per-sentence voice quality* from the demo (that IS representative), but expect flat delivery from raw paragraph input — the sentence-split wrapper is the real integration and a solved problem. Sources archived in chat 2026-07-13 (rhasspy/piper issues #492 & #199, HA core #156603 static-tail, Mintplex-Labs piper-tts-web browser build). **✗ KITTEN — EVAL NO-GO (quality), 2026-07-13 hands-on:** user tested it, "sounds like a blown speaker" — audible distortion, not merely a plain/robotic tone. Expected failure mode at 15M params (footprint bought at the cost of quality); it was only ever the size long-shot. Not usable for immersive narration; **dropped from the local-on-phone race.** Leaves **Piper** (with the sentence-split wrapper) as the front-runner for the `$0/char` local-on-phone slot, Kokoro as desktop-only, and Inworld/Cartesia as the hosted path. Revisit Kitten only if a materially better small model supersedes it. **🔧 PIPER SPIKE BUILT (2026-07-13) — `piper_test.html` (satellite, NOT in SW shell; open directly like bible_study.html).** Auditions Piper's real voice on real Ammut prose WITH the sentence-split + punctuation-normalization wrapper the demo pages lack; A/B toggle for raw-vs-split; live per-sentence RTF + char/audio/synth metrics. Uses `@mintplex-labs/piper-tts-web` via esm.sh. **Harvest map (baked into the file as comments):** `normalizeForTTS()` + `splitSentences()` = ✅ lift VERBATIM into a shared provider-agnostic text-prep block in `tts.js` (helps Cartesia + native too); `loadPiper/ensureVoice/synth` = ✅ the Piper adapter core; Web-Audio scheduling = ◐ game already has it (`scheduleChunk` tts.js:214), written to mirror its shape. **VERIFIED WORKING:** library import, voice-model download+cache, sentence-split (6 units on the sample), Web-Audio wiring — all good. **⚠ BLOCKER surfaced = the real Piper integration cost, now concrete:** ONNX Runtime Web's WASM backend won't init in the sandboxed preview browser — first failed fetching ORT wasm from cdnjs (`ort-wasm-simd-threaded.jsep.mjs`), then under COOP/COEP cross-origin-isolation it failed on a `blob:` worker-module import. Root cause is env-specific (the preview Chromium has restricted network egress / worker policy); **the fix for a real deploy is to SELF-HOST the ORT wasm files + pin `ort.env.wasm.wasmPaths` (don't depend on cdnjs at runtime) and get the COOP/COEP combo right — exactly the "messy dependency plumbing" this row warned Piper would cost.** Could not verify audio in-sandbox (also can't judge voice quality by ear from here regardless — that's the user's call). **NEXT (user, ~1hr):** run `piper_test.html` in a REAL desktop Chrome served over http (`npx serve` in the repo folder → open the localhost URL; plain serve = ORT single-thread path, cdnjs reachable on a normal network) and LISTEN — does Piper's per-sentence voice hold up for narration? That's the gate before the ~½-day in-game integration. If yes → harvest the text-prep pass first (needed for every provider anyway), then the Piper adapter. **⛔ SPIKE OUTCOME — DEFINITIVE (2026-07-13, verified in the user's real Chrome, NOT just the sandbox; supersedes the "probably works on a real machine" guess above):** could NOT get CDN-loaded Piper to synthesize; the failure chain is fully characterized and it's an ARCHITECTURAL conflict, not a config tweak: ① the `@mintplex-labs/piper-tts-web` lib hardcodes `ort.env.wasm.wasmPaths` → `cdnjs/onnxruntime-web@1.18.0/`, but the `.jsep.mjs` WASM-glue file doesn't exist for 1.18 on any CDN (glue files began at 1.19) → *fixed* by pinning ORT **1.19.2** + locking wasmPaths to `jsdelivr@1.19.2` via a getter (verified the files 200 there). ② But 1.19.2 ships **ONLY a threaded jsep build** (no non-threaded single-file — `ort-wasm-simd.jsep.*` 404s), and the threaded binary needs `SharedArrayBuffer` even to instantiate → **requires cross-origin isolation** (COOP/COEP). Without COI it hangs; `numThreads=1` just 404s on the missing non-threaded file. ③ WITH COI on, ORT spawns its pthread **Worker from the cross-origin esm.sh module** → COEP blocks it: `SecurityError: Failed to construct 'Worker': script at esm.sh/onnxruntime-web… cannot be accessed from origin`. **Conclusion: Piper-in-browser needs the ORT runtime + worker + wasm all SAME-ORIGIN (self-hosted) AND served with COI headers. CDN-loading ORT (esm.sh/jsdelivr) is fundamentally incompatible with the threaded-build + cross-origin-worker + COEP combination.** Same-origin self-hosting of a bundled lib+ORT effectively needs a **BUILD/bundler step — which collides head-on with the project's core "no build step, no npm dependencies" tenet.** So the real #41 decision if Piper stays in: (a) accept a build/bundle step for the TTS layer (violates a core principle — user call), (b) find a pre-bundled same-origin ORT distribution or a Piper wrapper that inlines a non-threaded WASM (no worker, no COI), or (c) an older non-threaded ORT path. **What DID work & harvests regardless:** the text-prep (`normalizeForTTS`/`splitSentences`) ran correctly (split the sample into 6 units) — still lift it into `tts.js` for every provider. **To judge Piper's VOICE QUALITY now (the actual gate, decoupled from integration):** paste a few Ammut sentences into a hosted Piper demo (e.g. offlinetts.com/app/piper) and listen per-sentence — no engine work needed. Spike file `piper_test.html` retained as the documented scaffold + harvestable text-prep; it will synth once ORT is self-hosted same-origin. **🎧 VOICE VERDICT (2026-07-13, user judged on rhasspy piper-samples + demo.html):** Piper voice quality = **"passable — worth exploring"**; needs a proper LONG-TERM listen (many turns of real narration) to judge fully. **Piper stays a LIVE contender** (not eliminated like Kitten). Run-on/punctuation = confirmed non-issue (our sentence-split wrapper, split-logic already verified; only the gapped-audio-by-ear proof is pending a working synth). **NEXT STEP to enable the long-term listen = get the spike actually producing audio = solve the same-origin ORT problem WITHOUT a build step.** Promising no-build path: **vendor onnxruntime-web (its `.mjs` + worker + `.wasm`) AND the piper lib as local files, wire the bare `onnxruntime-web` specifier to the same-origin copy via a native `<script type="importmap">`** (import maps are a browser feature, no bundler → keeps the zero-build tenet), served over http with COOP/COEP. If import-map vendoring can't force the lib's internal ORT import same-origin, the fork from the SPIKE OUTCOME note stands (accept a scoped TTS build step, or find a Piper wrapper that inlines a non-threaded WASM). This is the concrete task when Piper work resumes. **✅ SOLVED (2026-07-13, spike now SYNTHESIZES) — the no-build vendoring path WORKS; NO build step, NO cross-origin isolation needed.** The winning recipe (all proven live in `piper_test.html`): ① vendor `@diffusionstudio/vits-web@1.0.3` (`vits-web.js` + `piper-DeOu3H9E.js`) AND `onnxruntime-web@1.18.0` (ESM `ort.wasm.min.js` + the **non-threaded** `ort-wasm-simd.wasm`/`ort-wasm.wasm`) as LOCAL same-origin files (~20MB total, mostly the 2 wasm binaries); ② a native `<script type="importmap">` redirects the bare `onnxruntime-web` specifier (vits-web loads it via `await import("onnxruntime-web")` — bare, so import-map-redirectable) to the local copy → **ORT is same-origin, killing the cross-origin-worker wall**; ③ import the same local ORT in the page and LOCK `ort.env.wasm.numThreads=1` + `wasmPaths="/…/ort/"` via ignoring setters (vits-web sets `numThreads=navigator.hardwareConcurrency`→32 and `wasmPaths`→cdnjs/1.18; both must be overridden). numThreads=1 selects the non-threaded `ort-wasm-simd.wasm` → **no worker, no SharedArrayBuffer, no COI** → runs on a plain http server. **Live metrics (Lessac, real Ammut paragraph, 692 chars → 6 sentences):** 38.1s audio synth'd in 18.9s = **2.0× real-time overall**, **2.5–4.5× per sentence** after a one-time ~9s WASM-compile on the first call. Phonemizer (`@diffusionstudio/piper-wasm` jsep) + voice model (HuggingFace) still load cross-origin — fine on a plain (non-COEP) server. **So the earlier "needs a build step" fork is RESOLVED — vendoring + import map keeps the zero-build tenet.** For the real in-game integration this is the shape: vendor ORT+vits same-origin (into the repo/app shell), single-thread, locked local wasmPaths; the sentence-split text-prep still harvests into `tts.js` first. Spike vendored files currently in session scratchpad (throwaway) served by a local static server; **user is now unblocked for the LONG-TERM voice-quality listen.** **⚠ TEXT-PREP FIX (2026-07-13, found in first real use):** single-threaded Piper scales badly on long utterances — a ~500-char run-on sentence (commas, no period) synth'd as ONE unit hangs 30s+. Fixed in the harvestable `splitSentences`: added `MAX_UNIT=220` cap + `packLongUnit()` that sub-splits an over-long sentence on clause boundaries (`,;:`) then word-wraps if needed. Verified: the 521-char hang → 3 units, completes ~3–4.5× real-time. **This cap is MANDATORY for the in-game text-prep** (GM prose routinely has long sentences) — harvest it with the splitter. **✅✅ PIPER VALIDATED — GO (2026-07-13, user heard it working on a proper localhost tab):** verdict = *"Is it the greatest VO? No. Passable, especially considering the cost? Spike `piper_test.html` + the vendored-files recipe = the working blueprint. Cache-Control` detach working), correct JS/WASM content-types, shell no-cache, deployed APP_VERSION current; side-catch: `/sw.js` served a duplicated `no-cache, no-cache` token (overlapping `/*.js` rule appends without a detach) — fixed in `_headers` same day. ② `tnd-piper-v1` cache-bump survival: strong local evidence — the piper cache held exactly the 4 vendor files alongside the versioned shell cache through multiple CACHE bumps (k) in the sandbox SW; the real-deploy confirmation remains a formality on the next deploy. ③ Piper synthesis re-verified in the #2 pre-flight (same-origin loads, OPFS voice download to 100%, clean stop). ④ **Hosted-tier pricing RESOLVED (verified on official pages, Fable spot-checked Inworld):** Inworld Realtime TTS-2 = $25/1M chars on-demand with a self-serve ladder ($20/$17.50/$15/$12.50 → "$5/1M" enterprise), ~70 min free ≈ **$3.00/hr of active play** vs Cartesia Sonic-3.5 at $37–50/1M ≈ **$4.49–6.00/hr** — Inworld beats Cartesia 35–45% and takes the paid-quality slot recommendation when the hosted adapter gets built (OpenAI tts-1 ~$15/1M and Google Chirp 3 ~$30/1M could not be verified on primary pages this pass — recheck if either becomes a real contender). **Still human:** the long-term LISTEN (the actual gate) and cross-device on the deployed origin. **🎧 First phone listen (2026-07-16, via Car Mode): "voice is ok, nothing to write home about — we can tweak it."** First tweak shipped (v1.310): **tiered pause gaps** — Piper renders no punctuation pause itself, so `splitSentences` gains a Piper-only `commaSplit` mode (breaks at every `, ; :`, tags each unit's `end` type) and scheduling maps each type to its own independently tunable constant: `PAUSE_COMMA`=0.15 / `PAUSE_COMMA_CLAUSE`=0.22 / `PAUSE_FULLSTOP`=0.44 (doubled from 0.22 by user call) / `PAUSE_PARAGRAPH`=0.88 (tts.js, one block). Next listen judges the comma-breath quality — all four values are one-line tweaks. | M | Sonnet | ⚙ Piper shipped; pause tiers v1.310 — **user verdict 2026-07-16: punctuation timing "more or less perfect" (values locked)**; v1.320-v1.322 killed the iOS long-passage tab-crash — ROOT CAUSE found at v1.322: upstream vits-web predict() created a NEW ORT InferenceSession from the FULL ~60MB model on EVERY sentence and never released it (per-unit memory growth; Chrome absorbed it, Safari killed the tab at the same unit count every read). Vendored file now carries a T&D session-cache patch (one session per voice, release on switch) AND v1.323 caches the PHONEMIZER instance (upstream also spawned a fresh Emscripten module + espeak-ng-data FS per sentence — the second faucet, why v1.322 got further but still died; callMain reuse verified live, loud per-call fallback if a future build cannot re-run main; run-tests tripwire guards BOTH patch markers against re-vendoring). **⚠ v1.324 — THE DELIVERY TRAP + proof (the wasted-tries lesson):** /vendor/piper/* is cache-first from the PERMANENT tnd-piper-v1 SW cache (+immutable header), so the v1.322/v1.323 vendored patches NEVER RELIABLY REACHED installed clients — including the verification sandbox itself (the SW re-registers on load and re-served the stale module; instrumentation caught it: 43 phonemizer wasm instantiations per 43 units on the stale runtime). Side effect: synth much faster after unit 1 (no per-sentence model re-parse). Phone re-test CONFIRMED at v1.324. **v1.327 — the phone-silence class (native worked, Piper+Cartesia silent, desktop fine):** diagnosed from the engine split — both broken engines schedule on the shared AudioContext, native does not. Two iOS facts: a persisted voice-ON flag means a reload never runs toggle(), so the ctx is born OUTSIDE a gesture at first narration (iOS: suspended, resume denied); and iOS has a third state "interrupted" that every suspended-only check missed — even a real Test tap scheduled onto a stopped clock. Fixed: _resumeCtx handles both states everywhere; one-tap document-level unlock armed whenever voice-on persists across a load; visibility-return resume; synth entries REQUIRE state===running or refuse LOUDLY (toast + native fallback for the line — no more silent scheduling); Voice Settings shows a live "Audio:" state line (phone-readable diagnosis). Refusal path proven in preview with a suspended ctx whose resume() lies (the iOS behavior). **v1.328 — the second layer (phone screenshot: Audio RUNNING + r2 loaded + still silent):** a running ctx discarded at the OUTPUT stage = iOS device-level muting — bare WebAudio plays in the "ambient" session category, which the physical ringer/silent switch mutes while speechSynthesis ignores it (the exact native-works split). Fix: primeAudioSession (the Car Mode playback-category primer that exists for exactly this) now runs for ALL WebAudio narration — wired at toggle-on and both synth entries (idempotent). Mute-switch-immune + consistent BT routing. Verified: primer coexists with Piper playback in preview. **v1.328 CONFIRMED WORKING on the phone after an app kill+relaunch** — the kill also cleared a WEDGED pipeline (stranded _playing latch from a native fallback whose iOS completion events never fired; Test taps queued silently behind the phantom). **v1.329 makes that class self-healing:** Test pre-empts via stop() (clears any wedge), per-unit native stall watchdog (forced advance + loud warn — _playing can never strand silently), paused-engine resume() kick, and the Audio diag line shows pipeline state (speaking/idle +N queued); still open: long listen + cross-device; hosted recommendation: Inworld. **v1.334 (Fable piper-audit 2026-07-17, findings #3+#4):** ① primer now survives a voice off/on cycle — _closeCtx left a stale _primerSrc from the closed ctx, so primeAudioSession short-circuited and the v1.328 playback-category claim silently vanished after any 🔇→🔊 (fix: _closeCtx clears it + primeAudioSession verifies the primer is on the CURRENT ctx); ② user pause honored by all anti-wedge machinery — tap-unlock and visibility-return no longer auto-resume a user-suspended ctx (audio restarted by itself while the button showed ▶), the native stall watchdog defers while _paused (keyed on user intent ONLY, so the iOS engine-paused wedge still force-advances), and skip/stop clears the pending watchdog (a live one resurrected the cancelled chain up to ~30s later). **v1.335 (findings #1+#2, vits-web r3):** ① download integrity — upstream S() saved whatever fetch returned, so an HF 404/rate-limit page could be cached to OPFS as the .onnx forever (stored() reports it "downloaded", every predict fails, nothing re-fetches); non-OK now THROWS before anything is written; ② phonemizer .wasm/.data (~18MB) vendored to /vendor/piper/phonemize/ — tndLocate pointed at jsdelivr, which the SW never caches (cross-origin), silently breaking the offline claim; ③ hang rider: the fallback phonemizer promise had no reject path — a load failure hung predict() forever. Tripwire (run-tests.js) extended: r3 markers + on-disk phonemize assets + PIPER_RUNTIME_REV↔TND_VITS_PATCH rev parity (the delivery-trap class is now test-enforced). **v1.336 (finding #5, vits-web r4):** delivery rev extended to ALL permanently-cached piper files — the ?tnd= query previously covered only vits-web.js itself; piper-DeOu3H9E.js (relative import drops the parent's query) + the phonemize wasm/data now ride `TND_DEP_REV` (r1), ort.wasm.min.js rides its own ?tnd=ort-r1 on the import-map entry, and the ORT .wasm binaries (wasmPaths prefix — can't take a query) are documented RENAME-on-change. Tripwire enforces the dep-rev queries in both vits-web.js and index.html. Known residual: stale ?tnd= revs accumulate in the never-purged PIPER_CACHE (a few hundred KB per rev — cosmetic). **v1.337 (finding #6, found DURING live verify; vits-web r5):** upstream `I()` fired the OPFS write (`p()`) without awaiting it — download() resolved while the ~78MB commit was in flight, so the FIRST predict after a voice download read the not-yet-committed (empty) OPFS file → ORT "No graph was found in the protobuf" → whole item failed to native. Timing-dependent (fast connection = reliable repro on desktop; explains any first-download flakiness on phones). Fixed by awaiting the write. **Live-verified end-to-end on localhost:** fresh OPFS → download → immediate first synth OK, warm synth + playback + clean drain OK, runtime line r5, phonemizer served same-origin (zero jsdelivr requests in the network log). **v1.338 (audit round 2, finding #7):** Piper's comma-split treated the [,;:] inside "1,000 gold" / "3:30" as clause boundaries — spoke "one … zero zero zero gold" on every formatted number. `mergeDigitClauses` (tts.js) re-joins digit-tight junctions in BOTH splitSentences commaSplit and packLongUnit; test-first (4 new engine tests, 3 red pre-fix). **v1.339 (findings #8–#11):** ⑧ boot prewarm — a reload with voice-ON never ran toggle(), so the ~9s WASM compile landed inside the first narration of every returning session; loadSettings now prewarms, download-GATED (engine+voice warm only if the voice is already on disk — never a surprise 60MB pull); ⑨ `_piperSerial` mutex — voice ensure/download and every predict (incl. prewarm's) run one-at-a-time on the shared single-threaded wasm session (epoch discards stale results but never prevented overlap; two racing ensures each started a 60MB download); ⑩ WebAudio stall watchdog (`_armCtxWatch`) — the v1.329 native watchdog's missing twin: a mid-read iOS ctx interruption with no hide/show (BT route change, screen on) froze narration silently with _playing latched; now resume-attempted + ONE loud toast + tap unlock armed; ⑪ `_piperVoiceComplete` — stored() only proves an .onnx file EXISTS; now both files must exist non-empty (catches the 0-byte relic a tab kill leaves mid-first-download, and the missing .json that made "downloaded" voices need the network at narration time); any gap re-downloads both. **v1.340 (findings #12–#16, vits-web r6):** ⑫ `navigator.storage.persist()` requested once per session at voice-ensure (OPFS eviction witnessed live — a 78MB voice vanished between desktop sessions; denial logged, non-fatal); ⑬ download stall watchdog in vendored S() — no data/response for 30s aborts LOUDLY (a stalled fetch used to hang ensure forever, and the #9 mutex would have blocked all later Piper ops); ⑭ pagehide/beforeunload mark the crash crumb done — deliberately closing the app mid-read no longer false-alarms "narration died" at next boot (real crashes fire neither event); ⑮ PIPER_CACHE GC — caching a new ?tnd= rev deletes superseded same-path entries; ⑯ truthful per-voice sizes (HEAD-checked: mediums 60MB, LibriTTS R 75MB, Ryan high 115MB) in blurbs, modal info line, and the download toast. **v1.341 (final deep-dive, finding #17 — the last high-severity):** a device where AudioContext can't be created (`_ensureCtx()` null — no WebAudio, or the 22050Hz constructor throws) DROPPED every Piper/Cartesia line silently (`_drain()` skip — no speech, no toast, voice showing ON); both paths now fall back to native with a console warn, same idiom as their other failure branches. Crumb now carries cumulative synth count + session uptime (`pc`/`up`) and the boot toast prints them, so the NEXT death (if any) discriminates on-phone: similar cumulative count = ratchet (lower threshold / mid-read recycle / worker isolation next); low count on a giant read = per-read peak. Voice Settings runtime line shows live synth counters. Vendor-check ⑤ in run-tests.js guards the patch pair. | 57 | **Reveal-commitment gap — identity reveals never update structured memory** (graduated from UA40; the t378 woman-in-bronze incident, case study preserved in saves t359/t378). Established canon coexisted with a stale pre-reveal hedge in `knowledge[]` AND a fully forked NPC entry — the prompt served CONTRADICTORY canon and the model amplified it. Three design gaps: (a) merge/alias emission at reveal moments leans on GM judgment (the historically spotty class); (b) **knowledge has no supersession** — extraction appends, a reveal never retires the hedged fact it obsoletes; (c) revelations are the defining-moment class engine triggers CANNOT detect — the strongest argument for the deferred `[CORE_MEMORY:]` GM tag (#40 Phase 2). Queued (bounded 6, deduped) → `buildConsumableNudge` (api.js, 9th NOTE_BUILDERS entry): one item per turn, asks the GM to check its OWN narration and emit the battle-tested tag "or leave the sheet alone — do NOT invent a consumption" (the false-positive escape, the C-scenario-validated soft-nudge shape); cooldown latch `CONSUMABLE_NUDGE_COOLDOWN`=6 written on fire (an ignored nudge means "not spent" — re-nagging would railroad a false decrement); combat-silent WITHOUT consuming. **Pre-review (confidently resolved, recorded per the amended clause):** companions included (same class, same tags); no spend-verb gate (soft wording + cooldown bound the loose match instead); the player-driven "use item" sheet affordance deferred as complementary UX (doesn't fix the observed class — the player didn't notice the ghost either). Never auto-decrements — the only write path remains the sole parser; stable half untouched (notes ride the message); strip/doc hashes untouched (no new tag). 10 engine tests (524 total) incl. the t582 regression fixture; live spot-check on v1.308: t582 scenario queues → note renders → latch → re-fire silent. **Live validation pending:** a real session where a consumable is spent untagged — expect the nudge next turn and a correct single decrement. **#60b (v1.384) — the shipped check was FEEDING ON ITSELF; root-caused on the t881 corpus.** TLDR: the check's "not consumed" answer had nowhere to go but the story text, and the story text is what the check reads, so it kept re-triggering on its own denials. The "STRICT OUT-OF-CHARACTER MODE" wording was already forceful and was never the problem; the gap was entirely on the input side. **Structural guarantee (preserved, load-bearing):** the TT path never calls `applyMuts` and never calls `logTranscript` — it CANNOT mutate state or the narrative record. That, not prompt wording, is what makes "never GM" true. **Ratified design (user, 2026-07-20):** ① single GM persona throughout, in-world or not, never the second-person narrator voice — no register-switching (rejected: needs the model to classify the question first, a new failure mode). ② **NEVER INFER — report what state holds** — a GENERAL rule, NOT per-topic branches. Worked example is dates: no calendar exists today, so "days to the solstice" returns NOT TRACKED; **[#73](#) owns that gap** and when it lands #76 needs ZERO changes. An `if (question is about dates)` branch would be a bug twice over — a test asserts the source contains no such branch. ③ memory tiers + question-keyed `ragRetrieve` over the transcript (the rat-kabob vendor exists ONLY in raw transcript — no summarizer would promote it). ④ a rolling TT history SEPARATE from `sessionLog`, so follow-ups work while TT chatter never reaches story context. ⑤ **rules = name index + matched entries** — all 169 bible names always (~1–2k tok) so "not in the bible" is honest, full `capBibleLine` canon only for names the question matches; rejected whole-bible at 169 entries / ~15k tok / ~$0.045 per question, cacheable only in bursts (TT gets its own cache breakpoint and goes cold between questions). ⑥ **app help DERIVED from the rendered File menu** — `buildFileMenus` builds HTML imperatively so there is no object to walk, and the rendered menu is a better source anyway: it cannot hold an independent stale copy of the truth. Covers everything menu-reachable including the trigger case (File ▸ ⚠ Report bug). Known gap: non-menu UI (tabs, input row, sheet interactions) is undocumented — revisit only if real use shows it matters. **Architecture:** new `table-talk.js`, its own `buildTableTalkPrompt()`; deliberately does NOT reuse `buildSysPrompt`'s stable half — a second differently-shaped prefix would silently kill gameplay prompt-cache hits (the UA5 failure mode: no functional symptom, pure cost regression). | M | Fable | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.387 (2026-07-20).** New `table-talk.js` (after api.js; added to index.html, the sw.js app shell, and dev/load-engine.js). game.js TT path sends `{noHistory:true,kind:"tabletalk"}` (own Usage bucket) and runs `cleanTxt`. **Verified:** 731 engine assertions green (incl. the stable-half byte-identity tripwire — `buildSysPrompt` untouched); a dedicated 24-assertion suite exercising FAILURE cases (Message resolves to the real 120ft + its "not telepathy" clause; solstice yields NOT TRACKED with no date branch in the source; an unknown capability produces NO canon block; TT log never touches `sessionLog` or `transcript`; caps hold at 50 pushes); live browser check of the DOM-dependent menu derivation (⚠ Report bug present, icon-only "×" row filtered), no-game state does not throw, zero console errors. Full prompt ≈1,823 tokens. **Absorbs #74 entirely.** ⚠ Model-compliance caveat: "answers are DIRECT, no GM'ing" is a prompt instruction — unverifiable except in the field. Watch it in real use. |

Three findings settled it. **(a) LiveKit does no STT itself** — transcription lives in the Agents framework, which needs an always-on server worker plus server-signed JWTs, so none of it is reachable from a static, no-build, no-npm, ES5.1 client; the whole benefit list is conditional on an architecture we don't have. **(b) Every gap it was raised to close is already closed in-repo** — the iPhone Safari path by the `MediaRecorder → whisper-1` fallback in stt.js (v1.342), fantasy proper nouns by `sttCorrectNames` (v1.330), and multiplayer by hot-seat on ONE device (#1 — there is no networking to attach voice to yet). The research lanes were briefed off a stale CLAUDE.md and spent most of their effort solving solved problems; **the CLAUDE.md `stt.js`/`tts.js` rows were corrected in the same commit as this row.** **(c) Cost inverts who pays** — today the player spends their own key; LiveKit bills the *developer* ~$0.60/player-hour in agent-session fees dominated by connection time rather than speech, reopening exactly the ownership decision #9 already settled on on-device Piper for $0. One genuine capability gap survives — coordinated turn-taking / barge-in for Car Mode — and LiveKit's own issue tracker has that closed *not planned*, so adopting it wouldn't fix it either. Re-open triggers listed in §8 of the findings doc (the honest one: if #1 ever grows real cross-device networking, the transport question is worth re-asking — but as transport, not as STT). | S (investigation) | Sonnet | 🚫 **Closed — investigated and DECLINED** (2026-07-21, user call) — see [DOC/liveKit_findings.html](DOC/liveKit_findings.html) |

That helps the symptom; it does not give subtasks. <br><br>**Design questions for the rework:** does a task become its own section (heading + prose + a small status table) rather than a table row, which would remove the one-physical-line constraint entirely? Does the history (the shipped-version log that makes #9 huge) belong in the task at all, or should it move to a changelog and leave the task describing only what is LEFT? That last one is probably the biggest single win and the least work. <br><br>**Constraint worth respecting:** the current format's one virtue is that it is greppable and machine-checkable, and the linter has caught real corruption from at least two writers (viewer exports, hand/model edits). | 87 | **The Piper phonemizer's reuse patch fails open into the v1.323 leak, permanently and silently.** TLDR: `tndPhonemize` (vendor/piper/vits/vits-web.js) caches ONE Emscripten phonemizer module and re-drives it via `callMain`; if any call throws it sets `tndPhon.broken = true` and falls back to building a FRESH 16MB module per synth — which is exactly the v1.323 leak the cache exists to prevent. **Nothing ever resets the latch**, so one bad sentence downgrades the rest of the page's life, and the only complaint is a `console.warn` a phone cannot show. <br><br> **Found in a lab, not the field (2026-07-22, while making ORT memory observable for B9):** the new wasm probe counted **27 phonemizer modules over a 100-synth soak** where the design allows one, with `memory access out of bounds` in the console. ⚠ **The TRIGGER is unproven on real narration** — it fired on long synthetic word-salad sentences from the soak generator, so it may be an artifact of that input. The latch, the missing reset and the silent downgrade are plain in the code regardless of what trips them. <br><br> **The field answer is already instrumented:** v1.416 puts the module count on the narration-death crumb as `pn`, so the next real crumb says whether this happens in play. **Decide on that evidence before coding.** Remedies, cheapest first: (1) reset the latch after N synths or on the next read so one fault is not permanent; (2) make the downgrade LOUD (toast + `reportError`), since a silent fallback into a known leak class violates the no-silent-failures policy; (3) find and fix what makes `callMain` fault on re-entry. ⚠ Any `vendor/piper/*` edit MUST bump `PIPER_RUNTIME_REV` (r9 today) or the permanent SW cache eats the patch. | S–M | Fable | 🚫 **CLOSED 2026-07-27 (user ruling): parked pending field evidence — no `pn`>1 crumb in 5 days of watching, and #90 (v1.435, shipped 3 days into the watch) moved connected-play synthesis to the server, shrinking the local phonemizer's trigger surface to offline reads and ladder degrades.** The lab trigger was synthetic word-salad input, always suspect. The watch outlives the row: the `pn` counter rides every narration-death crumb permanently (v1.416), so a real fault emails its own evidence in and reopens this with a case attached. Mechanism stays recorded in DOC/BUGS.md ▸ B9 (2026-07-22 entry). |

| 90 | **Server-side TTS M1 — the B9 architectural close (R1, mandated GO 2026-07-24).** TLDR: B9's root cause is an iOS per-page synthesis-work budget no client arrangement survives; the fix moves synthesis to the Fly server — self-hosted Piper, same 19 voices, identical audio, zero client wasm work, so B9 dies on every device incl. Design: [DOC/DOC_server_tts.html](DOC/DOC_server_tts.html). | M-L | Fable | ✅ **M1 SHIPPED (v1.435, 2026-07-24, built by Fable).** All five decisions ratified at build start: **D1** session-token auth (unconnected players keep the local ladder) · **D2** second Fly app **`tnd-tts`** (game server stays 256MB; TTS sized independently) · **D3** degrade toast once per session · **D4** Kokoro benchmark-gated (→ #91) · **D5** 3GB volume. **Server half** (server repo `tts/`, deploy `cd tts && flyctl deploy --ha=false`): Hono app, `POST /api/tts` {text, voiceId, rate} → audio/wav; warm `piper` daemon per (voice, rate) — spawn-per-request pays ~1-3s model load, so daemons persist with LRU cap 3 + 10min idle kill; the `--json-input` stdin protocol's stdout path-print is the completion signal, FIFO-aligned even across timeouts (a timed-out entry stays queued so later responses can't shift); 2 timeout strikes kill+respawn the daemon; voices download HF→volume on first use behind an allowlist mirroring `PIPER_VOICES` (the endpoint can't be used to fill the volume with arbitrary models); auth = proxy `/auth/me` with a 10min memo (one auth round trip per read, not per unit — known coarseness: a logged-out token works ≤10min, the gate protects CPU, not revocation latency); same per-IP rate limiting as the game server; `TTS_TEST_SECRET` ops lever (DEV_LOGIN_SECRET pattern) for deploy smoke tests — set, verify, UNSET. **Client half** (tts.js, off drift surface): `TTS_PROVIDERS.server` + `TTS_LADDER = server → piper → native`; `getEngine()` RESOLVES (connected+healthy → "server", else "piper" — offline devices byte-identical to #9 behavior, the 3 constant-piper engine tests pass unchanged); `_speakServer` = the `_speakPiper` unit loop with predict() → fetch (same splitter/speaker-maps/manual-WAV-decode/scheduler/backpressure/shared-epoch); ANY unit failure hands the WHOLE remainder down the ladder via the queue (the governor's handoff pattern — one timeout, never a per-unit stall crawl) + 60s retry memo; `prewarmServer()` health probe wakes the auto-stopped machine off the critical path; the governor never meters the server tier (no client work to budget) and keeps guarding the local tier forever; `storageAdapter.authHeader()` exposes the assembled header, never the raw token; Voice Settings shows a server-tier status line (off/active/degraded-with-reason). **Tests:** 810 green (+5 server-tier engine tests: resolution, D1 no-token, degrade window + backdated retry, D3 single toast, enqueue shape) + a 4-point SERVER TTS source contract in run-tests.js (zero-wasm, no governor contact, remainder handoff, ladder order). **v1.436 field fix (same day, from the user's first phone session):** the 🔋 governor latched because Fly parked the machine after ~2min idle and real turns idle longer — so nearly every read's first unit paid the measured 10.17s cold chain (machine boot + auth proxy cold-starting the GAME server too + daemon spawn + model load), 170ms past the 10s unit timeout → whole read degraded to local Piper → budget spent. Four-part close: ① fly.toml `auto_stop_machines = "suspend"` (RAM-intact sub-second resume — warm daemons + auth memo survive); ② `TTS.prewarmServer()` on the send gesture (game.js, beside the B10 recoverAudio tap) so the machine wakes while the GM is thinking; ③ first-unit timeout 15s (`SERVER_TTS_TIMEOUT_FIRST_MS`; units 2+ stay 10s); ④ diagnosability — ▶ Test auditions via the server tier when it's up (a local Test booted wasm + spent governor budget for nothing; no eviction prompt either, nothing downloads), and a connected page reading below the server tier now stamps a `tts-server-skip` crumb + console.info (silent-local reads were climbing the budget with no signal — the no-silent-failures gap). Contracts ⑤⑥ pin the Test routing + the send-tap prewarm. |
| 91 | **Server-TTS M2 — Kokoro-82M behind the same endpoint (quality upgrade, benchmark-gated).** TLDR: with M1 (server Piper, #90) live, the same `POST /api/tts` can serve Kokoro-82M (Apache-2.0) — markedly better than Piper mediums, ~24-26 English voice packs, voice BLENDING for per-character voices. **📄 SPEC'D 2026-07-24: [DOC/DOC_kokoro.html](DOC/DOC_kokoro.html)** — decisions K1-K6: **K1** runtime = in-process `kokoro-js` in a worker_thread (no daemon protocol, quant ladder fp32→q4; python sidecar only as fallback) · **K2** the voice id IS the engine selector (`kk_` prefix routes; allowlist discipline as M1; blend syntax `kk_mix:` reserved day one, mixer UI deferred to M2.5) · **K3** the real client work: tier-aware voice resolution (`voiceTier`/`narratorVoice(tier)`/`characterVoiceId(char,tier)`) so a server-only `kk_` id can never reach local `predict()`, the ladder handoff converts to the piper narrator, and the snap guard can't eat a kk_ campaign pin — the one drift-adjacent surface (speaker-map resolution), annotate for /fable-review · **K4** the D4 benchmark concretized: `tts/bench-kokoro.mjs` on the LIVE box via fly ssh, 30 real-length units × q8/fp16/fp32, pass = sustained median RTF ≥1× at an ear-checked quant, with the piper daemon as the comparison row · **K5** box sizing decided BY the benchmark (stay 1GB w/ piper LRU 3→2 / 2GB ≈ +$5/mo / performance-1x needs explicit ratification) · **K6** curated en-only catalog via a voice_picker pass, gender-tagged from day one (the #9⑨ lesson). Milestones M2.0 benchmark → M2.1 server → M2.2 client → M2.3 field validation (default flip = user call) → M2.5 blending. **D4 (ratified): ships only after the benchmark passes.** <br><br> ⛔ **M2.0 RAN 2026-07-24 — D4 GATE FAILED, Kokoro does NOT ship.** Measured on the live box: at 1GB (the shipped config) the process **OOM-died before its first synthesis** — Kokoro needs ~275MB RSS and the box had ~119MB free with the session's three warm Piper daemons resident. Scaled temporarily to 2GB (K5's candidate tier) it ran, and the SPEED is the disqualifier: **RTF ≈ 2.1 at full burst** (D4 bar is ≤1.0), then a step-change collapse to **RTF 22.65 → 24.67 → 33.68** — one 7-second line took **3m56s**. Mechanism (evidence-backed): ~48s of continuous 100% CPU exhausts the `shared-cpu-1x` **burst balance**, then the machine throttles to baseline; it is a step, not a ramp, and loadavg stayed busy throughout, so it is not memory/GC. Piper never trips it at RTF 0.16. **RAM was a red herring; CPU is the wall** — no RAM tier fixes it, and even the best-case number is **13× Piper's cost per second of audio**. Box restored to 1GB, volume reclaimed, `tts/bench-kokoro.mjs` kept for re-runs. **K1/K2/K3/K6 remain valid and rebuildable** the day the CPU math changes (a faster quantization, a cheaper build, or ratified dedicated-CPU spend). Full record + the confirming burst test: [DOC/DOC_kokoro.html](DOC/DOC_kokoro.html). <br><br> ➡ **The benchmark surfaced a better route — see #94:** Piper's own **"high" voices are already in the shipped catalog** (`en_US-libritts-high` is ALREADY on the server volume, plus lessac/ryan/cori-high) — same engine, same daemons, zero architecture change. A high model costs more CPU than a medium (unmeasured) but the headroom is enormous: it could be **6× slower than medium and still clear the D4 bar**, where Kokoro needed to get 2× *faster*. A general style REGISTER is deliberately still allowed ("dressed for hard travel") because it survives a costume change and is useful to BOTH consumers; banning all clothing talk would over-correct. | 99 | **BUG: "Replace appearance" did not replace the appearance.** Reported 2026-07-27 from the "appearance read from the portrait" panel. **Two distinct defects behind one symptom.** **① Display (every subject):** the write landed but nothing repainted the sheet under the portrait modal, so the old text stayed on screen until the sheet was closed and reopened — for the PLAYER the data had in fact persisted the whole time, which is why it read as "doesn't seem to" rather than a hard failure. **② DATA LOSS (sheet-less party members):** `showNpcSheet` builds `npcSubject` as a THROWAWAY object literal when the NPC has no `charSheet`, and the handler wrote `c.appear = desc` straight onto it — the description went into an object discarded on close, `saveAll()` then saved state nothing had changed, and a success toast claimed otherwise. Reachable in normal play: a `[PARTY_MEMBER:]` join creates an NPC with no sheet (exactly why the ✦ Generate Character Sheet button exists), and the avatar/portrait button renders for any party member. **Fix (v1.460):** a caller-supplied `setAppearance` seam, the same shape as the existing `getPortrait`/`setPortrait` pair. The default writer targets the player and repaints `#cs-modal` (a separate element from `#portrait-modal`, so the portrait modal stays open). `showNpcSheet` supplies one that writes `wsNpc.charSheet.appear` and repaints the NPC sheet; with no charSheet it REFUSES with a toast pointing at Generate — without a sheet nothing would ever READ an appearance anyway (the sheet renders it from charSheet, and `doRender` pulls party descriptions from charSheet), so a "successful" write would be a second silent lie. A refusal deliberately leaves the generated text on screen rather than discarding work that cost a vision call. | S | Any | ✅ **Done (v1.460, 2026-07-27).** New **APPEARANCE WRITE CONTRACT** in dev/run-tests.js pins the seam (no raw `c.appear=` write; the NPC writer targets charSheet; the refusal message survives) — **sabotage-verified:** restoring the original raw write trips it by name. 858 green. All three subject paths verified live: player persists + repaints, NPC with a sheet writes to the real home, NPC without a sheet refuses loudly and calls saveAll zero times. Related: #98 (the prompt those descriptions come from). <br><br>**v1.461 follow-up — repaint on CLOSE, not per write (user's call, and the better seam).** User confirmed the data was updating and pinpointed the remaining symptom: "the text didn't update in the already opened sheet… I think we just need to rerender the character sheet when the edit portrait UI closes." Correct — and measurement showed why v1.460's per-write repaint was the wrong seam: the portrait modal renders at **z-index 400** over the sheet's **300** (verified live via `elementFromPoint`), so a mid-flow rebuild is invisible to the user, and it only covered the appearance handler while portrait and framing edits still relied on the avatar-only `refreshFn`. Now `pmClose()` does `modal.remove()` then, if anything was actually written, `refreshSheet()`; `modalShell`'s `onClose` routes through `pmClose` so the × button and an outside-click repaint too, not just the three internal close paths; a `_pmDirty` flag keeps a look-and-leave close from rebuilding the sheet and resetting its scroll; and `showNpcSheet` supplies its own `refreshSheet` instead of the writer tearing down its parent modal mid-flow. **Verified live through the REAL buttons** (not stubs): open sheet showing old text → Describe → Replace → close → sheet current with no reopen, old text gone; same for an outside-click close; a clean close rebuilds nothing (0 calls). Contract extended to pin the mechanism (pmClose repaint, onClose routing, all six dirty-marking write paths) and **sabotage-verified** — removing the repaint trips it by name. **Effort:** S < 1h · M 1–3h · L 3–8h · XL multi-session · — done or no work currently needed

**Tier** (which model the task *needs* — how smart, where Effort is how long): **Fable** = architecture / high judgment / expensive-to-get-wrong · **Opus** = substantial builds with real design decisions · **Sonnet** = well-specified builds · **Any** = mechanical work or real-device/play testing · — = done/merged (needs nothing)

**⛨ Drift-protection policy (2026-07-09):** any task touching the drift-abatement surface — however small — is **Fable-tier by decree**, critically reviewed BEFORE work starts and thoroughly checked after. The surface definition + protocol live in [Fable_UberAudit.md](DOC/Fable_UberAudit.md) ▸ "Standing policy"; also in CLAUDE.md ▸ Dev workflow.

| 3 | Portrait storage bloat / localStorage quota — portraits (base64, ~22–52KB each) dominate state size and are multiplied several ways: (a) **companion portraits stored 2×** — both `npc.portrait` and `npc.charSheet.portrait` hold the same image; (b) **`snapshotActiveCamp()` duplicates the entire state** (incl. all portraits) into `tnd_camp_<id>_*` on EVERY `saveAll`, so the active campaign is stored twice (~884KB doubled in the Ammut save). With multiple campaigns this multiplies → real mobile-quota risk (iOS Safari is strict). When quota is hit, `saveCore` catches it, toasts "storage full", and falls back to in-memory `_m` — so the save silently doesn't persist across reload. **Fixes:** dedupe companion portrait (single source); skip the redundant snapshot of the *active* campaign (only snapshot on switch-away — `tnd_core_v10` already is the active state); consider storing portraits in their own dedup'd localStorage keys so snapshots don't copy them. ## Long term goals

Where this is meant to END UP. These are **not blocked on engineering** — they're blocked on money, assets, a business decision, a partner, or on development settling down enough for the work to stay true. They live outside the backlog so they stop reading as work you're behind on, but they belong in writing, because they're the things today's architecture has to stay *compatible* with. When a decision here lands, it usually collapses several backlog rows at once.

**The destination (user, 2026-07-18):** self-publish if it can carry itself; otherwise sell to a real game studio and use their resources "to really make it sing." Everything below is written so that either exit stays open.

| # | Task | Status |
|---|---|---|
| L1 | **Real audio — recorded ambience, not synthesis (the ASSETS).** Tavern room tone, wind through the peaks, waves against a dock, a murder of crows. This is the *atmosphere* content half; the system that houses and plays it — plus the action-sound earcons — is **L7** (Audio Library). The synthesized earcons that shipped as a placeholder sell UI feedback fine but will never sell atmosphere; recorded audio is what fills that gap. | Budget for assets (or a studio's audio library) |
| L2 | **COMMERCIAL blueprint catalog** — premium campaign packs as a subscription perk or one-off purchases: entitlement checks, purchase flow, paid-tier gating. **Only the commercial half lives here** (split 2026-07-18, user call): the *alpha starter set* went BACK to the backlog as **#44①**, because testers need content to choose from long before anyone needs a checkout. | The business model (L3) — it defines what an entitlement even is |
| L3 | **The commercial shape itself** — subscription vs one-off vs sale. | A decision |
| L4 | **Make it sellable to a studio** — the diligence surface, not a feature. | Nothing — this is hygiene, done continuously |
| L5 | **Game document — TECHNICAL.** The design/architecture document: how the anti-drift stack actually works (tag table, memory tiers, prompt construction + the stable/volatile cache split, RAG, blueprint/skeleton lifecycle), the schemas, and above all the WHY behind each decision. Audience: a developer inheriting the codebase, or a studio's technical due diligence (feeds **L4**). Glossary ([DOC_technical_terms.html](DOC/DOC_technical_terms.html), #24) is its appendix. | The ARCHITECTURE settling — a lower bar than the feature set, so this one is writable earlier than L6 |
| L6 | **Game document — PLAYER.** The manual a player reads: character creation, what a turn is, suggested actions, the sheet and party panel, quests and companions, voice/Car Mode, hot-seat multiplayer. Plain language throughout — **the state tags and memory machinery must stay invisible here**; this explains the GAME, never the engine. | Staged, NOT one end-of-development drop (user call 2026-07-18) — see the two stages opposite |
| L7 | **Audio Library — design and implement (the SYSTEM).** A real audio system for the game: the infrastructure to house, manage, and play sound, plus the produced assets that fill it. Absorbs the two audio backlog items moved here 2026-07-20 — **action sounds / earcons** (old #7) and **ambient environment sound** (old #13). ⚙ **A synthesized placeholder already shipped and is LIVE in the app** (`sound.js`, v1.356→v1.376): WebAudio earcons + a 10-click material palette (wood/stone/metal/glass/bone/leather/parchment/…); wired today as bone = the general toast poke, glass = the attention-getter (quest offer · level-up · defining moment · combat start), with a "UI sounds" toggle + a ♪ Sounds… audition modal in the Admin drawer. **User verdict on synthesis:** *"a little 8bit, not really the feel we're going for"* — it sells UI feedback fine, but never atmosphere. The destination is real produced/recorded audio + a proper library to play it; the *atmosphere* content half lives in **L1**. Full synth-build history is in git (v1.356/v1.373/v1.374/v1.375/v1.376). | Blocked on budget/assets (like L1). Synth placeholder shipped & live; the real library needs produced audio. |

## Architecture decisions

### Age is cosmetic-only (2026-08-10)
**Decision:** Character/companion `age` is a display-and-render field ONLY. It is deliberately injected into ZERO gameplay prompts — not the player identity header, not the PARTY MEMBER SHEETS block. Only the image-render prompt writers read it (scene render `doRender`, the portrait paths). Do NOT "fix" the missing injection.

**Why:** injecting raw years would force the GM — and every campaign — to model cross-ancestry age semantics: 67 is an elderly human but a coming-of-age elf (user: "I really don't want to have to worry about balancing a 70 year old elderly human and a 70 year old coming of age elf"). Age-driven fiction belongs in the fields the GM already reads: `appear` prose ("lined at the corners of the eyes"), ancestry blurbs ("a long-lived southern lineage"), conditions. Field evidence: Daeris played ~1,100 turns at age 67 with zero gameplay effect, and that was the desired behavior — the number only surfaced when the user read her sheet.

**Guard note:** this LOOKS like the #46/#61 write-path-with-no-read-path defect class (companion conditions/relationships were written but never injected, and were rightly fixed as bugs). It is not — it is a ruled non-injection. A constraint comment sits at the partyBlock in api.js so a future audit doesn't pattern-match it back into the defect class.

### Stable entity IDs vs. names-as-keys (2026-06)
**Decision:** Keep names as the internal key for now. Do NOT introduce hex/UUID entity IDs as a standalone refactor. Fold identity-by-ID into Multiplayer (#1) or the server-canonical model (subscription) — whichever lands first — where it becomes a near-free side effect instead of a project.

**The load-bearing constraint:** the GM is the dominant consumer of identity and it speaks in *names*, not IDs. Every state tag is `[NPC:Veyra|…]`, `[COMPANION_HP:Lyra|…]`, `[RELATIONSHIP:Veyra|…]`. The model can't reliably emit or track hex IDs, so name→ID resolution would still have to run on every tag at the parse boundary. IDs therefore don't *delete* `resolveNpcName()` — they demote it to a name→ID lookup we still own. The fuzzy part (aliases, "the merchant" vs. "Veyra", misspellings) lives in that layer either way.

**What IDs actually buy (narrow):** (1) two entities can share a display name simultaneously — twins, a reused "Guard," two Kaels — which aliases genuinely can't disambiguate; (2) rename/surname (#21) becomes a one-attribute flip with all edges surviving.

**What they cost (broad):** every `memory.npcs` key, `worldState.npcs[].name` comparison, relationship `entity`, graph edge, `findCompanionChar`, the save schema (v10→v11), plus a migration for every save already in players' localStorage — in an ES5, no-test codebase.

**Asymmetry = the answer:** broad cost, narrow payoff, *because the name-resolution layer stays regardless.* So IDs only pencil out when something else (multiplayer name collisions; server primary keys) needs them anyway and is already touching the save format.

**Today's 80/20:** make the existing alias/resolution layer (`[NPC_ALIAS:]` / `resolveNpcName()` in memory.js) the canonical identity mechanism — rename = register old name as alias of new. Covers #21 with no schema change. Optional low-regret hedge: start stamping an unused `id` on character/NPC nodes at creation so future ID-keyed code has them — but only worth it once #1 is real (otherwise YAGNI).

### JSON dictionary vs. SQLite for game state (2026-07-04)
**Decision:** Stay on the three JSON object trees (`worldState`/`sessionLog`/`memory`). Do NOT convert storage to SQLite as a standalone project. Explored, not advocated — recorded here for revisitation when the triggers below fire.

**Why staying is right today:** every read site is a synchronous property access (hundreds of touchpoints, ES5, no build step); query-on-read SQLite means async-everywhere and a near-total rewrite, so realistically the in-memory trees would stay and SQLite would be just a persistence mirror — collecting only quota + write-granularity benefits that IndexedDB provides without a ~1MB WASM dependency, cross-origin-isolation headers (OPFS), or breaking the `file://` play tab. The GM never sees the storage format — every drift/memory-quality problem lives in the selection/rendering layer, identical either way. The forensic workflow (node-load a `.tnd`, replay RAG, refile events — the whole 2026-07-04 session) depends on saves being one greppable JSON file.

**The two effects that genuinely tempt (both separable — adopt per-subsystem, never "convert the dictionary"):**
1. **FTS5 for RAG** — `ragRetrieve`'s hand-rolled IDF is a poor man's BM25; SQLite FTS5 gives real BM25 + indexes over the transcript, same no-API/deterministic character as Phase 1. Likely a better-fitting RAG Phase 2 than embeddings.
2. **Changeset-based sync** — the server is already SQLite; the session extension produces row-level changesets = the grown-up answer to whole-blob last-writer-wins (Known issue #5) and the multiplayer (#1) prerequisite.

**Revisit triggers:** RAG Phase 2 gets green-lit (→ consider FTS5 for the transcript index only); multiplayer/server-authoritative state starts (→ consider changesets server-side); or localStorage quota actually bites in the wild (→ IndexedDB first, not SQLite). *Quota DID bite 2026-07-18 (bug B4, iPhone at the iOS 2560K-char ceiling) — addressed WITHOUT IndexedDB by snapshot eviction ("Remove local" behind a confirmed cloud copy) + active-campaign de-dup (v1.363, see DOC/BUGS.md B4); IndexedDB remains the escalation if a single campaign ever outgrows the quota on its own.*

### Subscription model (2026-06)
**Decision:** Traffic and Dragons will ship as a subscription service. Users will not bring their own API keys.

**Rationale:** Broader audience requires abstracting away the Anthropic API key. Subscription revenue funds the API costs and server infrastructure.

**What this means architecturally:**
- All Claude API calls move server-side. `callGM()` in `api.js` will hit the T&D server instead of `api.anthropic.com` directly.
- The server holds the Anthropic key and proxies requests. It also handles rate limiting and usage tracking per subscription tier.
- `anthropic-dangerous-direct-browser-access: true` header goes away entirely.
- GitHub OAuth is already in place on the server — add subscription tiers on top of it.
- Migration path is clean: `callGM()` is a single function. Pointing it at the server unlocks the whole model.
- Client wraps in Electron for desktop app distribution. File System Access API works natively in Electron.

### Provider adapter & forward compatibility (2026-06)
**Decision:** Build swappable-LLM support (#11) as a clean provider abstraction, ChatGPT first. The abstraction is forward-compatible with the subscription model — it is *not* throwaway work despite the BYO-key vs. server-key tension.

**The shape:** a provider object — `{ id, label, endpoint, headers(key), buildBody(msgs, sys, maxTok), parseResponse(json) }`. `callGM()` selects the active provider and calls `buildBody`/`parseResponse`. NO `if(provider==="openai")` branches sprinkled through the code.

**Why it survives the subscription pivot:** the subscription model moves `callGM()` server-side with the Anthropic key on the server — which superficially looks like it obsoletes client-side multi-provider. It doesn't: (1) the server will need the *same* provider-routing table to offer model choice across tiers, so the abstraction transfers from client to server almost verbatim; (2) until subscription ships, BYO-key multi-provider is a real feature for current power users. Build the abstraction once, move it server-side later.

**Why ChatGPT first (de-risk, not just lowest-effort):** the #1 unknown for *all* multi-provider work — client or server — is whether a non-Claude model reliably emits the state-tag grammar (`[NPC:…]`, `[COMPANION_HP:…]`, the trailing `*You could…*` line, JSON-only `summarize()` output). The OpenAI adapter is the cheapest probe of that question. If GPT respects the tags, the whole #11/#12–15 line is green-lit; if it mangles them, far better to learn it in one evening than underneath a finished Car Mode. OpenAI's `messages` format is nearly identical to Anthropic's, so the adapter is small and the signal is almost pure "does the model follow the tag contract."

**OUTCOME (v1.32, confirmed live):** de-risk landed **positive, with one caveat**. gpt-4o handles everything structural (response parsing, JSON `summarize()`, the suggestion line — though it drops the `*asterisks*`, so `parseActions` gained an un-asterisked fallback). The gotcha: gpt treats *inline state tags as optional* — it narrates "you pay 5 gold" without emitting `[GOLD:-5]`, because OpenAI models are trained against leaking bracket-codes into prose. **Fix that worked:** a per-provider `reinforce` string (a forceful tag-discipline suffix) appended in `callGM()` for gameplay turns, OpenAI only — Claude stays lean. Live-verified across 3 consecutive gold turns on both input paths. **Implications:** (1) the abstraction earned its keep — the entire fix lived in the provider object; (2) every future non-Claude provider (#13–15) will likely need its own `reinforce`, now a known cheap step; (3) if reinforcement ever proves insufficient for a weaker model, the backstop is an **extraction pass** — a second `summarize()`-style call that pulls tags from the prose (structured extraction is the one thing every model does reliably).

### Server & services shape — ✅ DESIGNED + RATIFIED (2026-07-11)
**Status:** [SERVER_ARCHITECTURE.md](DOC/SERVER_ARCHITECTURE.md) is the authority (UA32 survey + UA33 design; all 7 §8 decisions user-ratified 2026-07-11: no free tier at launch · billing provider deferred, hard stop + top-up packs · data-free-forever lapse · Google login next, never passwords · state authority = staged path C with early shadow-parse; UA19 absorbed into its §6 Phase 2). The server is `traffic-and-dragons-server` on Fly.dev (GitHub OAuth, campaign + character + state sync, better-sqlite3 on one Fly volume — no Turso). Build order = that doc's §9; step 1 (git init + secret rotation + backup restore test + 5 cheap fixes) is unconditional and FIRST.

**The shift:** sync backend → **API gateway + account system + billing**. The server stops being optional.

**Open questions to resolve before building:**
- **LLM proxy:** server holds the Anthropic (and other-provider) keys, proxies `callGM()`. Needs streaming passthrough, per-tier rate limiting, usage metering (tokens in/out per user per turn), and abuse/runaway-cost guards. This is where the provider-adapter table (above) lands server-side.
- **Billing:** Stripe? Tiers (free trial / monthly / annual)? Metered overage vs. hard caps? Tier → model mapping (e.g. free = Haiku, paid = Sonnet/Opus). How does a lapsed subscription degrade — read-only, or locked?
- **Auth evolution:** GitHub OAuth is the current isolation boundary. Subscription accounts must replace/augment it (#2 note). Email+password? Keep OAuth as a login option but make the subscription the entitlement? Migration path for existing GitHub-keyed campaigns.
- **State authority:** today the client owns `worldState`/`memory` and syncs up. Does the server stay a dumb blob store, or become authoritative (needed for multiplayer #1 and anti-cheat)? Authoritative state is a much bigger build but unlocks multiplayer + server-canonical entity IDs.
- **Cost model sanity:** a single long campaign turn can be ~2–4k tokens system prompt + response; summarize() spikes to 2k output. At scale this is the dominant cost — metering and prompt-size discipline are load-bearing for unit economics, not nice-to-haves.
- **Deploy/scale:** Fly.dev single-region today (`flyctl deploy --ha=false`). Multi-region? DB scaling (Turso edge replicas)? Secrets management for N provider keys.
- **Electron desktop wrapper:** how does the desktop build authenticate to the same subscription backend?

**Next step (a future session, not tonight):** draft `SERVER_ARCHITECTURE.md` — survey the existing server's actual endpoints/schema first, then design the gateway + billing + auth layers against it. Same planning-doc pattern as STORY_COMPILER.md.

### Narrative knowledge graph (2026-06)
**Decision:** Build a structured NPC relationship index injected into every system prompt.

**Rationale:** As sessions get long and chapters compress, the GM loses fidelity on NPCs — hallucination and character drift become frequent. A machine-readable relationship web gives the GM a stable anchor that survives context compression better than prose summaries.

**Design:**
- NPC↔NPC edges via `[NPC_LINK:]` tag, stored in `memory.npcGraph.edges[]`
- Factions via `[FACTION:]`, `[NPC_FACTION:]`, `[FACTION_REL:]` tags
- `buildNpcGraph()` injects compact block into system prompt
- Faction display in world state sidebar, NPC faction membership on NPC sheet

### File System Access API for campaign folders (2026-06)
**Decision:** Use `showDirectoryPicker()` to let users set a campaign root folder at game start. All exports route into organized subfolders (`saves/`, `logs/`, `renders/`, `characters/`).

**Rationale:** Browser can't auto-create folders without user permission. One prompt at game start is less disruptive than prompting mid-game. Chrome-only but acceptable given target audience.

### Campaign name field (2026-06)
**Decision:** Add explicit campaign name field to Review step (step 7) of character creation, defaulting to character name.

**Rationale:** `worldState.campName` is the stable identifier used in all file exports and folder naming. Previously defaulted silently to char name which meant renaming a character didn't update file naming. Now set intentionally at campaign creation.

### Legacy characters (2026-06)
**Decision:** 5% chance per session load that a past player character from another campaign appears as an NPC.

**Rationale:** Rare enough to be surprising and feel meaningful. Once per campaign per character to prevent repetition. Sourced from other campaign slots in localStorage — no separate storage needed.

### OAuth for file:// origin (2026-06)
**Decision:** Replace postMessage with server-side auth ticket polling.

**Rationale:** Chrome blocks `postMessage` from `https://` to `file://` origin. New flow: server creates short-lived ticket in `auth_tickets` table after OAuth, client polls `/auth/ticket/:ticket` to claim it. Also listens for postMessage as fallback (works on Netlify). Ticket auto-expires after 5 minutes.

### Echo detection — belt-and-suspenders for #31 (2026-06)
**Status:** Parked — try the prompt rule first (v1.112), build this only if repetition persists.

**Concept:** After receiving a GM response, scan the last N responses for repeated descriptive structures (age comparisons, size similes, "older than X" patterns). If detected, append a one-shot transient note to the next system prompt: *"Your recent narration reused [pattern]. Vary your approach."* Same transient-marker pattern as `recentSwitch` / `recentlyLeft` — injected once, auto-cleared after 1–2 turns.

**Why it's worth noting now:** the prompt rule tells the model what to avoid but can't enforce it — if the model ignores the rule (especially non-Claude providers), echo detection is the backstop. Lightweight: a few regex scans on `sessionLog` in `sendAction`, no API cost, no new tags.

### Module system → Campaign designer (2026-06)
**Decision:** Folded module system (#5) into campaign designer (#18).

**Rationale:** Module system (alternate campaign settings) is naturally a subset of the campaign designer feature. No point building them separately.

### Character import flow (2026-06)
**Decision:** Import Character shows preview modal with two options: "Play as [name]" (new campaign) and "+ Add as companion to current campaign".

**Rationale:** Adding a character as a companion is a natural use case especially in multiplayer prep. Companion is added to `worldState.npcs` with full `charSheet` and introduced into the current scene via `sendAction`.

### Community blueprint sharing — scrapped (2026-07-08)
**Decision:** We will **NOT** build open community blueprint sharing (the public-browse angle once sketched in #15). The **personal** cloud library (your own blueprints, your own devices) stays; **curated official blueprints (#44)** is the sanctioned content path. The `public` column already on the server `blueprints` table stays **dormant** — no browse-public endpoint, no `public` flag ever flips for user content (leaving the column is harmless; ripping it out is a needless risky migration).

**Why — the moment strangers' blueprints flow into other players, three boundaries break, and only two are fixable by code:**
1. **Prompt injection into the GM.** Blueprint `rules` / NPC notes / location descriptions inject straight into the system prompt, so a hostile blueprint is hostile *instructions* (jailbreak, coaxed harmful output). Under the subscription model where **we** hold the API key, it's also a token-cost abuse vector against our own infrastructure.
2. **Stored XSS.** Model/user-derived text renders via `innerHTML` (incl. transcript replay on every reload); markup in a shared NPC name/desc executes in *other users'* browsers.
3. **Moderation + legal — the unbounded one.** UGC means offensive/illegal content, harassment, and IP infringement (our own dev fixtures are copyrighted adventures). Sanitization (#22) closes 1 and 2 — bounded engineering. **Moderation is a permanent staffing + legal commitment with no code fix**, and an un-curated firehose directly contradicts the family-car-trip positioning.

**The asymmetry that decides it:** the payoff (a content firehose) is a beautiful dream for an ideal world, but the cost is ongoing moderation liability shouldered by a small operator, and the worst downside — XSS, harmful content — lands on **innocent players**, not just us. Curated official blueprints (#44) capture the "there's great content to play" value *and* a revenue stream with **zero UGC surface**. That trade is decisively better.

---

## ⚠ Clear for Release

Things added for development/testing, or that are fine for a hobby build but NOT for a commercial launch. **The moment you add anything temporary, log it here** so it's never shipped by accident. Clear every row before release.

| Item | Where | Action before release |
|---|---|---|
| **Prose-voice author names** | `AUTHORS` in `data.js`; `showProseModal()` menu (`ui.js`) | Living/recent-author names (Gaiman, Abercrombie, Dinniman, Muir, Clines, Wells, Abnett, Rice) as a marketed feature risk right-of-publicity / trademark issues in a paid product. **Rename to descriptive style labels** ("Spare & Mythic," "Grimdark & Lean," "Breakneck LitRPG," etc.) — keep the directives unchanged; optional quiet "inspired by." Legal sanity-check. |
| **↻ Re-roll button** | `#reroll-btn` (topbar, `index.html`); `rerollLast()` (`game.js`); wiring (`ui.js`) | Testing aid for A/B-ing prose voices (regenerates the last scene with no turn/state side-effects). Decide if it stays as a player feature or gets removed before launch. |
| **Starting XP "tester override"** | Review step (`#rv-start-xp`, `index.html`); `confirmChar`/`rvSyncXp` | Lets a tester start at arbitrary XP. Labeled "(tester override)". Hide or remove for release (starting level alone should drive XP). |
| **Licensed-IP blueprint fixtures** | `rise_of_the_runelords.blueprint` (Paizo), `planescape_torment.blueprint` (Wizards of the Coast) in `samples/` | Fine as personal-use dev fixtures; **actual infringement inside a paid product.** Remove from the shipped build / repo before commercial launch; replace with original example blueprints. Same bucket as the author-name renames above. |
| **Client-side / BYO API keys** | api-screen key entry (`index.html`); `apiKey`/`providerKeys` (`AKK`/`PKEYS_K`); fal.ai key (`FAL_KEY_K`); Cartesia key (`tts.js` `KEY_K`); all provider keys | **Security + model blocker.** Today users paste their own keys, stored in localStorage and sent direct from the browser — fine for a hobby build, unacceptable for a paid product. Under the subscription model ALL calls (LLM `callGM`, fal.ai render, Cartesia TTS) move server-side behind the authenticated T&D server, which holds the keys; drop the `anthropic-dangerous-direct-browser-access` header. Remove the client key-entry UI + localStorage key storage. See "Subscription model" + "Server & services shape" in Architecture decisions, and the future `SERVER_ARCHITECTURE.md`. |
